### EV-002 - Escrow initiateFund re-funds an already-FUNDED (or RELEASED/REFUNDED) milestone: double brand debit, orphaned hold, releasable twice

- **Finding:** Escrow initiateFund re-funds an already-FUNDED (or RELEASED/REFUNDED) milestone: double brand debit, orphaned hold, releasable twice
- **Severity:** P0
- **Evidence Score:** E4 (register E5)
- **Confidence:** 85
- **Status:** **CLOSED — FIXED IN COMMITTED CODE de249ed, VERIFIED FROM THE COMMIT.** On the audited RC 115f698 itself, still VERIFIED as originally reproduced; verification verdict CONFIRMED / provenance COMMITTED (the RC itself was never patched — a later commit on the same branch was). The fix was first built and verified in an uncommitted checkout (2026-09-19), then committed as `de249ed` (parent `fb6d267`, merged onto the other session's `F-0873` campaign-pool work) and independently re-verified from that commit by Meera. See the Remediation log above for the full investigation, pre-merge reviews and commit-level evidence. New evidence score/confidence: E5/92.
- **Investigation answer (2026-09-19, per the owner's question):** escrow in this codebase is already just an internal ledger hold (LedgerEscrowBackend, brand wallet -> platform clearing wallet) — there is no backend on/off switch for it, and no separate Razorpay/regulated escrow path exists to switch to yet, matching the standing decision to defer real escrow until volume justifies it. The owner's "happened once when the webhook wasn't there" hypothesis is **NOT the cause of EV-002** — that matches an older, already-fixed gateway-plus-webhook double charge (commit `8deb097`, 2026-07-27). EV-002 itself has no webhook involved at all: it is a synchronous double-debit through `initiateFund` whenever a fresh Idempotency-Key reaches an already-funded milestone (reload, second tab, or a retry after a lost response). Confirmed by reproducing the double funding on a real MySQL 8 database with zero webhook/Razorpay code in the path (see Remediation log).
- **Evidence:** [meera-db:MEERA-DB-02] EscrowService.initiateFund:212-341 dedupes only by escrowHoldRepository.findByIdempotencyKey(client key). assertContractActiveForMilestone:388-440 checks contract signatures and collaboration != CANCELLED but never milestone.getStatus(). applyFunding:479-500 calls milestone.markFunded(hold.getId()), and PaymentMilestone.markFunded:141-145 overwrites escrowHoldId unconditionally. escrow_holds (V9) has only uq_escrow_idem(idempotency_key), with no uniqueness on milestone_id. Scratch unit test (clone of EscrowServiceTest.initiateFundRecordsApplicationHistoryEvent with the milestone pre-set to FUNDED via markFunded and a fresh key) completed without an exception, verified 1 ledger post and printed 'MEERA-AUDIT-REPRO: second fund accepted; milestone.escrowHoldId now=01M2WE36TAT8380D272F78HW9C'. || [vikram-pay:VIK-P1-02] EscrowService.initiateFund (EscrowService.java:212-335) dedupes only on the client Idempotency-Key (:261-274). assertContractActiveForMilestone (:388-434) checks signatures and cancellation but never milestone.status or milestone.escrowHoldId. applyFunding (:495-503) calls milestone.markFunded(hold.getId()), which unconditionally sets escrowHoldId and status=FUNDED (PaymentMilestone.java:141-145), even from RELEASED. Release uses per-hold keys 'release:'+holdId (:913), so the new hold is releasable again, and the ledger will debit the brand and credit the creator a second time. The only guard is FE-side (deal-payments-tab.tsx:194 canFund); useEscrowFund.ts:203-205 mints a new key per fresh submission.
- **Adversarial verification:** Traced the full lifecycle in the RC: initiateFund (213-333) dedupes only via client-controlled Idempotency-Key; assertContractActiveForMilestone (388-430), the only milestone gate for the fund path, checks contract signatures and collaboration CANCELLED status but never reads milestone.getStatus() (FUNDED/RELEASED/REFUNDED); EscrowController.fund adds no further check; applyFunding (473-517) unconditionally re-funds and overwrites milestone.escrowHoldId (markFunded), orphaning the prior hold; V9__escrow_holds.sql has no UNIQUE/CHECK on milestone_id; releaseInternal (848-905) always resolves and releases whichever hold milestone.escrowHoldId currently points to, so a re-fund silently redirects release onto the new hold regardless of the old hold's already-RELEASED state. No existing test (EscrowServiceTest) covers re-funding an already-FUNDED/RELEASED milestone. This is a genuine, currently-reachable double-debit/double-payout defect on real money movement -- P0 is warranted (siding with meera-db over vikram-pay's P1). New evidence: Confirmed via direct code trace across initiateFund -> assertContractActiveForMilestone -> applyFunding -> releaseInternal, plus the DB schema (V9__escrow_holds.sql), that nothing anywhere in the stack checks milestone status before creating a new hold, and that release always follows whichever hold a re-fund most recently pointed the milestone at.
- **Location:** influora-api/src/main/java/com/influora/service/EscrowService.java:212-341, 388-440, 479-500; domain/entity/PaymentMilestone.java:141-145; web/EscrowController.java:68-89 | influora-api/src/main/java/com/influora/service/EscrowService.java:240-274,473-503; domain/entity/PaymentMilestone.java:141-145
- **Reproduction:** Scratch copy of influora-api, add EscrowServiceTest#meeraAuditReproMilestoneDoubleFund (milestone.markFunded('01HFIRSTHOLD000000000A') before initiateFund with key 'idem-fund-SECOND'), then run mvn -o test -Dtest=EscrowServiceTest#meeraAuditReproMilestoneDoubleFund.
- **Impact:** A brand that retries (new Idempotency-Key after a timeout, second tab or double submit) on the same milestone is debited again. The milestone now points only at the second hold, so the first FUNDED hold is never released by the milestone path, and the money stays locked in the clearing wallet until an admin intervenes. The same applies to a RELEASED milestone, which can be funded again.
- **Affected Features:** Escrow funding per milestone, Brand wallet balance, Milestone release, Escrow fund (POST /wallet/escrow/fund)
- **Agents:** meera-db, vikram-pay (finder models: opus); merged from meera-db:MEERA-DB-02, vikram-pay:VIK-P1-02
- **Recommended Fix:** In initiateFund's milestone branch, lock the milestone (add PaymentMilestoneRepository.findByIdForUpdate) and reject unless status == PENDING and escrowHoldId == null (409 MILESTONE_ALREADY_FUNDED). Optionally add a partial-uniqueness guard, e.g. a generated column funded_milestone_id = IF(status IN ('PENDING','FUNDED','FROZEN'), milestone_id, NULL) with a UNIQUE key. **Done (2026-09-19, verified in a fix checkout, not yet committed):** built almost exactly this way — `PaymentMilestoneRepository.findByIdAndWorkspaceIdForUpdate` (PESSIMISTIC_WRITE) plus a new `assertMilestoneFundable` gate returning 409 `MILESTONE_ALREADY_FUNDED` for FUNDED/FROZEN/RELEASED/REFUNDED, and `PaymentMilestone.markFunded` now refuses the same cases at the entity level. The DB-level UNIQUE index was deliberately deferred (new finding EV-179: prod may already hold a duplicate `milestone_id` row from the reported incident, which would fail the migration and block boot). Kavya and Priya both reviewed and APPROVE_WITH_NOTES'd the diff; required before commit: merge onto the other session's uncommitted `F-0873` change in the same file (one conflicting hunk, resolved and re-tested green), run the prod duplicate-row check before the index migration, and route a 409-handling/deterministic-idempotency-key follow-up to Ananya/Arjun on the frontend. See the Remediation log above for the full diff, review and test evidence. **Committed and verified from the commit (2026-09-19, `de249ed`):** the merge onto F-0873 is done — the commit lands both the milestone guard and the campaign-pool guard together. The prod duplicate-row check for the deferred UNIQUE index remains outstanding (EV-179, unchanged).
- **Verification Required:** Kabir/Neha: confirm on staging with a real DB that POST /wallet/escrow/fund twice with different Idempotency-Keys for one milestone produces two FUNDED escrow_holds and two ESCROW_HOLD debit legs. **Done (2026-09-19, fix checkout, not staging):** Meera ran exactly this reproduction against a real local MySQL 8 database. At baseline (`fb6d267`, unfixed) it produced 2 FUNDED escrow_holds and 2 ESCROW_HOLD debit legs, with no webhook involved, in every round tested. With the fix applied, the same two-thread race produced exactly 1 hold and 1 debit, and the losing/later attempt got a clean 409 `MILESTONE_ALREADY_FUNDED` with zero new rows. **Done at commit level (2026-09-19, `de249ed`, not staging):** Meera reran this reproduction directly against a clean checkout of `de249ed` — 7/7 real-MySQL races (milestone-level and campaign-pool-level) produced exactly 1 hold/1 debit each with a clean 409 for the loser, and the pre-fix baseline at parent commit `fb6d267` reproduced the double debit in the same harness with no webhook involved. A staging re-run with real sandbox keys, per Kabir/Neha's original ask, is still outstanding and remains a pre-push/pre-RC action item.
- **Finder disagreement noted at merge:** meera-db (opus) rates P0 with a scratch unit test proving the double post; vikram-pay (opus) rates P1 by code reading. Same defect; kept P0 on impact (real money double-debit / double-pay), not on confidence.
- **2026-09-19 final remediation — CLOSED:** The EV-002 fix (and the campaign-level pool guard closing EV-176/EV-177, and the locking-read fix for EV-178) were committed together to `feat/meera-creator-phase-e` as commit `de249ed` (`fix(escrow): one hold per milestone and per campaign pool - stop double funding (EV-002, EV-176, EV-177, EV-178, F-0873)`), parent `fb6d267`, **not pushed**. 13 files changed, 1545 insertions, 24 deletions: `PaymentMilestone.java`, `EscrowHoldRepository.java`, `PaymentMilestoneRepository.java`, `EscrowService.java`, plus 9 test files. The repo owner chose to merge onto the other session's previously-uncommitted `F-0873` work rather than defer it; the main-tree copies of the touched files were synced to the committed versions and no work was lost. Kavya and Priya's pre-merge review remains **APPROVE_WITH_NOTES**; their required process condition (merge onto `F-0873` before commit) is what this commit does. Pre-merge suite: `mvn -B clean test` — 3763 run, 0 failures, 0 errors, 22 skipped.
- **Meera's commit-level verify pass:** From a clean checkout of `de249ed`: 7 independent real-MySQL concurrency races (milestone-level and campaign-pool-level) never produced 2 holds or a 2nd debit — every race resolved to exactly 1 hold/1 debit with a clean 409 for the loser or a later fresh-key retry. A baseline race run against the parent commit `fb6d267` (pre-fix) reproduced the double debit in the same harness, with no webhook involved anywhere in the path.
- **Webhook hypothesis — reconfirmed at the commit level:** the historic incident the brand owner remembers ("happened once when the webhook wasn't there") matches a different, already-fixed incident — the gateway-plus-webhook double charge closed by commit `8deb097` (2026-07-27). EV-002 itself has never involved a webhook or a Razorpay order since that commit.
- **New finding EV-181 (P1):** a pre-existing, unrelated stall is not touched by this fix — every milestone fund now waits out `innodb_lock_wait_timeout` (~50s default) and silently drops its `FUND_ESCROW` application-history row, because `ApplicationHistoryService.record`'s `REQUIRES_NEW` insert (`ApplicationHistoryService.java:69`) waits on the same collaboration row `initiateFund` holds `FOR UPDATE` (`EscrowService.java` ~:525-527); a genuinely concurrent duplicate attempt then gets a raw MySQL 1205 (likely a 5xx) instead of the new clean 409. Observed by Meera on real MySQL: 0 history rows after 4 funds, "Could not write the application-history event" in the log. This stall plus a frontend timeout-and-retry is flagged as a plausible trigger for past duplicate-fund reports, possibly including the incident the owner referenced. Registered in section 11 and section 37; routed to Arjun/Vikram, out of EV-002's own scope.
- **EV-178 stays open (P3):** the plain non-locking SELECT F-0873 used for its dedupe read is fixed in `de249ed` (it is now the same locking read used for the EV-002 guard), but a real-MySQL REPEATABLE-READ snapshot proof via a Testcontainers integration test is still owed before this is fully closed — downgraded P2→P3 pending that proof.
- **Residual follow-ups (P3 unless noted):** a possible InnoDB gap-lock deadlock between the first pool-funds of two different campaigns racing each other (money-safe — InnoDB rolls one transaction back, no double debit); confirm via `EXPLAIN` that the new locking query uses `idx_escrow_campaign`; a replayed campaign-pool fund request returns the existing hold even if the requested amount differs from the original (product may prefer a 409 instead); frontend follow-up for Ananya/Arjun — treat `409 MILESTONE_ALREADY_FUNDED` as refresh-to-funded rather than a bare error, and make `useEscrowFund`'s idempotency key deterministic per milestone; **EV-179 remains open, unchanged** — the read-only prod duplicate-hold check must still run before any unique-index migration.
- **Overall decision unaffected:** remains **NO-GO** — EV-004, EV-005, EV-006, EV-007, EV-008 and EV-015 are still open P0s, and `de249ed`/`fb6d267` are committed locally only, not pushed, and no new RC has been cut from either.

### EV-015 - Brand (OWNER/ADMIN) can refund a FUNDED escrow hold to itself on demand, even after delivery/approval, bypassing dispute resolution

- **Finding:** Brand (OWNER/ADMIN) can refund a FUNDED escrow hold to itself on demand, even after delivery/approval, bypassing dispute resolution
- **Severity:** P0 (register P1, upgraded by verifier)
- **Evidence Score:** E4 (register E5)
- **Confidence:** 88 (register 85)
- **Status:** **CLOSED — FIXED IN COMMITTED CODE a76ea77, VERIFIED FROM THE COMMIT.** On the audited RC 115f698 itself, still CONFIRMED as originally reproduced (independent finders); verification verdict UPGRADED / provenance COMMITTED (the RC itself was never patched — a later commit on the same branch was). The fix was committed 2026-09-20 to `feat/meera-creator-phase-e` (on top of `de249ed`, part of a six-commit wave, not pushed) and re-verified from the commit — see the 2026-09-20 Remediation log entry above. New evidence score/confidence: E5/93.
- **2026-09-20 remediation:** `EscrowService.refundInternal` now refuses (409 `REFUND_REQUIRES_DISPUTE`) once any Deliverable of the hold's collaboration is past PENDING/DRAFT, or the collaboration itself is REVIEW_PENDING/REVISION_REQUESTED/COMPLETED; the read is now `findByIdForUpdate` with a fail-closed 404 on a missing collaboration. Kavya (QA Lead) independently confirmed by direct diff read that the commit touches only `DeliverableRepository.java`, `EscrowService.java` and 3 test files, and does not touch `adminRefundForDispute`/`adminReleaseForDispute`/`adminSplitForDispute`. Meera's runtime evidence: two funded holds were seeded directly in MySQL (funding needs Razorpay, a placeholder locally) — one with a PENDING deliverable, one with a SUBMITTED deliverable. `POST /wallet/escrow/refund` on the post-submission hold → 409 `REFUND_REQUIRES_DISPUTE`, confirmed afterward in the database that the hold is still FUNDED with `release_txn_id` NULL (no money moved); the pre-submission control on the same endpoint → 200 REFUNDED with a real release txn id. **Explicitly still open:** the approve-vs-refund deadlock this fix's own reviewers flagged — a brand can still decline to approve and decline to release, stalling a creator indefinitely, and this fix does not touch that separate stalemate; EV-021 (VIEWER-level approval) and EV-019 (approval never releases) are also untouched.
- **Evidence:** [swapnil:SWP-01] EscrowService.refundInternal (EscrowService.java:972-1013) checks only: workspace ownership, not already REFUNDED, assertEscrowNotBlockedByDispute (1804-1812), and status FUNDED. It never checks Deliverable status or whether the collaboration is past submission or approval. EscrowController.refund (EscrowController.java:152-166) requires only an OWNER/ADMIN brand member. Approval never releases money (SWP-02), so the hold is still FUNDED and refundable after approval. || [vikram-pay:VIK-P1-03] EscrowController.java:152-166 exposes POST /wallet/escrow/refund to any brand principal, and EscrowService.refund (:959-970) enforces OWNER/ADMIN only. refundInternal (:972-1015) checks workspace, REFUNDED no-op, dispute freeze (:981-986) and FUNDED state. It does not check collaboration status, submitted/approved deliverables, or release_condition, and it applies to milestone-backed holds (marks milestone REFUNDED, :1004-1012). The controller javadoc (:138-151) states the published refund policy allows no on-demand refund and that the route has no FE caller.
- **Adversarial verification:** EscrowService.java:943-1013 refundInternal only checks workspace ownership, REFUNDED idempotent no-op, dispute-freeze (assertEscrowNotBlockedByDispute, 1804-1810), and hold status FUNDED -- no deliverable/milestone approval check. MilestoneStatus has no APPROVED value; BrandDeliverableService.approve() only attempts release via tryReleaseOnApproval, gated by assertReleaseConditionSatisfied reading the milestone's release_condition, which defaults to ON_POSTED (EscrowService.java:1576) and is the ONLY value ever assigned anywhere (PaymentMilestone.java:249 is the sole assignment site, hard-coded ON_POSTED) -- so EVERY milestone leaves its hold FUNDED after mere APPROVAL, until POSTED. In that universal window, any brand OWNER/ADMIN can call POST /escrow/refund (EscrowController.java:151-165) using the SAME auth as any ordinary brand action -- no elevated/internal-only gate -- and reclaim funds despite approved, undelivered-to-creator work, with no dispute filed. EscrowController.java:140-148's own javadoc states the product policy is 'no on-demand refund' and that brand-reachable refund should go through dispute resolution only -- the code's sole enforcement of that policy is omitting a frontend button, not a server-side control. No test covers refund-after-approval. Given this is the default behavior for 100% of milestones (not an edge case), trivially reachable via a plain authenticated API call, directly contradicts documented payment-protection policy, and enables repeatable direct financial harm to creators, I upgrade from the finder's P1 to P0 rather than downgrading. New evidence: Traced PaymentMilestone.java:249 as the sole ReleaseCondition assignment site in main source, proving ON_POSTED is universal, not a corner case. Read EscrowController.java's javadoc showing the code itself documents the policy this implementation violates, and confirmed the refund endpoint's auth is identical to ordinary brand-action auth.
- **Location:** influora-api/src/main/java/com/influora/service/EscrowService.java:960-1013; web/EscrowController.java:152-166 | influora-api/src/main/java/com/influora/web/EscrowController.java:152-166; service/EscrowService.java:959-1015
- **Reproduction:** Brand funds a milestone -> creator submits -> brand approves (response released=false, NO_MILESTONE) -> brand POSTs /wallet/escrow/refund {escrowHoldId} -> hold REFUNDED and money back in the brand wallet.
- **Impact:** Creators can lose payment for content already delivered and approved unless they open a dispute first. This directly contradicts the 'secured payment' value proposition and is the biggest creator-trust risk in the marketplace.
- **Affected Features:** Escrow/secured payments, Creator payout, Deliverable approval, Escrow refund, Creator payment protection, Disputes
- **Agents:** swapnil, vikram-pay (finder models: opus); merged from swapnil:SWP-01, vikram-pay:VIK-P1-03
- **Recommended Fix:** In refundInternal, refuse the refund (409) when any Deliverable of the hold's collaboration is SUBMITTED/APPROVED/POSTED or later, and send the brand to the dispute flow. Alternatively, only allow refunds while the collaboration is pre-submission or CANCELLED by mutual termination.
- **Verification Required:** A runtime reproduction in a local/staging environment, plus a unit test asserting a refund after APPROVED is rejected.
- **Recorded disagreement:** Severity disputed: verifier UPGRADED to P0; Kabir and Rohan/Tejas cite P1. Decision uses P0.

### EV-005 - CampaignService.create() lets a brand create a campaign directly in ACTIVE with no funded-escrow check and no publish-fee charge

- **Finding:** CampaignService.create() lets a brand create a campaign directly in ACTIVE with no funded-escrow check and no publish-fee charge
- **Severity:** P0
- **Evidence Score:** E3 (register E4)
- **Confidence:** 88 (register 85)
- **Status:** **CLOSED — FIXED IN COMMITTED CODE 9bb8055, VERIFIED FROM THE COMMIT.** On the audited RC 115f698 itself, still OBSERVED as originally found; verification verdict CONFIRMED / provenance COMMITTED (the RC itself was never patched — a later commit on the same branch was). Committed 2026-09-20 to `feat/meera-creator-phase-e` (part of the six-commit wave, not pushed) and re-verified from the commit — see the 2026-09-20 Remediation log entry above. New evidence score/confidence: E4/93.
- **2026-09-20 remediation:** a single `CampaignActivationGuard` now gates every path to ACTIVE (create and update alike), so `create()` can no longer skip `requireFundedEscrow`/`chargeOnPublish`. Meera's live-probe evidence: `POST /api/v1/campaigns` with `status:"ACTIVE"` → 400 `CAMPAIGN_CREATE_STATUS_NOT_ALLOWED` ("A new campaign is saved as a draft. Secure the funds, then publish it."); the identical payload with `status:"DRAFT"` → 201, persisted as DRAFT (id `01M2YVKXB74NJNBKS9Y20ECB1V`). **Explicitly still open:** the exactly-once-publish-fee ledger invariant this fix is meant to guarantee is still unproven in any environment Meera ran in — `CampaignActivationLedgerIntegrationTest` is one of the 23 Testcontainers classes skipped locally (Docker down), and the commit message itself flags it as needing a Docker/CI run before the invariant can be treated as settled.
- **Evidence:** [blueprint-campaigns-discovery:CAMP-01] Full read of CampaignService.create() (lines 145-235) shows no call to requireFundedEscrow() or brandCampaignFeeService.chargeOnPublish() anywhere in the method, regardless of the incoming status. A repo-wide grep for both symbols returns exactly 2 call sites each, both inside update() and ConfirmLaunchExecutor's Meera confirm_launch tool — create() appears in neither. CampaignWriteRequest.status carries no bean-validation restriction, so a POST /campaigns body can set status=ACTIVE directly. CampaignActivationGatesTest (3/3 green this session) exercises ONLY the update()->ACTIVE path; there is no equivalent test for create()->ACTIVE. Both real product UI entry points (campaign-form.tsx's Publish button and brand-new-hype-campaign.tsx's default Launch submit) call api.campaigns.create with status:'ACTIVE' directly, not create-then-PATCH.
- **Adversarial verification:** Read CampaignService.java create() (145-235) end-to-end at the RC: CampaignWriteRequest.status is client-controlled with no bean-validation restriction to DRAFT (defaults to DRAFT only if the client omits it); the only status gate is validator.validateStatusForWorkspace, which (CampaignValidator.java:62-70) checks only workspace.verificationStatus == VERIFIED for ACTIVE and contains no escrow or fee logic. Confirmed requireFundedEscrow(...) and brandCampaignFeeService.chargeOnPublish(...) exist ONLY inside update() at lines 368-377, gated on transitioningToActive, with no equivalent calls anywhere in create(). Also checked CampaignActivationGatesTest.java: all three tests target the PATCH/update() path only, so create()-to-ACTIVE has zero regression coverage in the committed suite. A VERIFIED-workspace MANAGER/ADMIN/OWNER can POST /campaigns with status=ACTIVE directly, producing a live campaign with zero funded escrow and zero publish fee charged, exactly as claimed. New evidence: Confirmed no test in the RC exercises create() with status=ACTIVE; all activation-gate coverage is PATCH-only.
- **Location:** influora-api/src/main/java/com/influora/service/CampaignService.java:145-235 (create, no requireFundedEscrow/chargeOnPublish call) vs lines 368-377 (update()'s transitioningToActive branch, which calls both); requireFundedEscrow defined at lines 704-714, called only from update() line 369 and Confir
- **Reproduction:** See evidence; reproduce by reading the cited source lines at RC 115f698.
- **Impact:** A brand can publish a campaign (standard OR HYPE) straight to ACTIVE via the real 'Publish'/'Launch' UI — making it immediately visible and applicable to creators (CampaignSpecs.browsableForCreator only checks status==ACTIVE && !isPrivate) — while never funding an EscrowHold and never being charged the brand publish fee that BrandCampaignFeeService.chargeOnPublish exists to collect. This is a direct revenue-loss and escrow-policy-bypass path through the domain's primary, everyday create flow, not a hypothetical crafted-request edge case.
- **Affected Features:** Campaign CRUD/duplicate/analytics (/campaigns*), HYPE campaign flow (FE /brand/campaigns/new/hype, App.tsx:269)
- **Agents:** blueprint-campaigns-discovery (finder models: sonnet); merged from blueprint-campaigns-discovery:CAMP-01
- **Recommended Fix:** In CampaignService.create(), when the resolved status is ACTIVE, run the identical requireFundedEscrow(campaign.getId()) + brandCampaignFeeService.chargeOnPublish(campaign, workspace.getId()) sequence update() runs at its transitioningToActive edge (after save, inside the same @Transactional boundary) — or, simpler and safer, reject POST /campaigns with status=ACTIVE outright (400/409) and require every campaign to go through create-as-DRAFT-then-PATCH-to-ACTIVE, which is the only path that is actually gated today.
- **Verification Required:** Runtime: as a verified brand with zero EscrowHold rows and a Free-or-Pro plan, POST /campaigns with a valid body and status:'ACTIVE'; confirm the response is 201 with the campaign ACTIVE, that no EscrowHold row exists for it, that no WalletTransaction publish-fee debit was recorded, and that it now appears in GET /creator/campaigns (browsableForCreator).

### EV-004 - /internal/** service-token gate bypassed by a percent-encoded path; any logged-in user can refund Meera turn credits and forge assistant messages

- **Finding:** /internal/** service-token gate bypassed by a percent-encoded path; any logged-in user can refund Meera turn credits and forge assistant messages
- **Severity:** P0
- **Evidence Score:** E3 (register E4)
- **Confidence:** 82 (register 85)
- **Status:** **CLOSED — FIXED IN COMMITTED CODE be76665, VERIFIED FROM THE COMMIT.** On the audited RC 115f698 itself, still VERIFIED as originally found; verification verdict CONFIRMED / provenance COMMITTED (the RC itself was never patched — a later commit on the same branch was). Committed 2026-09-20 to `feat/meera-creator-phase-e` (part of the six-commit wave, not pushed) and re-verified from the commit with a live exploit-chain probe this time, not code analysis alone — see the 2026-09-20 Remediation log entry above. New evidence score/confidence: E4/93.
- **2026-09-20 remediation:** a new `RequestPaths.java` decodes and normalises the request path before `SecurityConfig`, `AuthRateLimitFilter` and `InternalServiceTokenFilter` match against it. Meera's live-probe evidence, with a real, freshly-issued brand JWT: `/api/v1/%69nternal/meera/turns/release`, plain `/internal/...`, `/INTERNAL/...`, `/./%69nternal/...` and double-encoded `/%2569nternal/...` all → 401, matching the no-token baseline; the same token gets 200 on `GET /api/v1/campaigns` and `GET /api/v1/wallet/balance`, so the gate is not a blanket deny. **Explicitly still open:** XR-PRIYA-01 (below) is not in this wave's close list and stays open pending its own re-verification, and EV-079 (service-token replay, needs a coordinated Python-side change) is untouched.
- **Evidence:** [kabir:KAB-02] InternalServiceTokenFilter.java:82-83: `String path = stripContext(request.getRequestURI()); if (!path.startsWith(INTERNAL_PREFIX)) { chain.doFilter(...); return; }`. The URI is raw and never decoded. AuthRateLimitFilter already decodes for this exact bypass class (its javadoc cites Kabir NEW-1), but this filter was never given the same fix. MeeraInternalController (e.g. :319 persistTurnWriteback, :354 releaseTurnCredit) never checks that the principal is InternalPrincipal/ROLE_INTERNAL_SERVICE. SecurityConfig.java:298-299 requires only authenticated() for /internal/**, which a normal user JWT satisfies. The on-behalf token is handed to the browser (MeeraSessionService.java:441-448, TurnResult.onBehalfToken). AICreditService.release (AICreditService.java:263-293) refunds any charged turnId in the workspace, once, with no check that the turn failed. Runtime probe against the real spring-w
- **Adversarial verification:** Independently opened InternalServiceTokenFilter.java, SecurityConfig.java, and MeeraInternalController.java at the RC. Confirmed the filter prefix-checks the raw, still-percent-encoded request.getRequestURI() (lines 82-83), and SecurityConfig falls through to bare .anyRequest().authenticated() for /internal/** at line 298 with no explicit role matcher (unlike /admin/** and /creator/**), and no HttpFirewall/UrlPathHelper override exists anywhere in the RC to close the raw-vs-decoded path mismatch against Spring Boot 3.3.5's default PathPatternParser-based MVC routing. Confirmed the two named routes (/messages, /turns/release) exist exactly as claimed at MeeraInternalController.java:319-372. Refinement: both routes sit behind a second independent gate, OnBehalfAuthResolver, not defeated by the percent-encoding trick alone -- but I found new evidence (MeeraSessionService.java:439-443, 'the browser forwards THIS as onbehalf_jwt') that the on-behalf JWT is legitimately returned to the browser during normal use, so a normal logged-in user already holds a validly-signed token for their own workspace/conversation, making the combined exploit (filter bypass + replay of one's own on-behalf token) concretely reachable for AI-credit-refund fraud and forged assistant messages, albeit scoped to the attacker's own account rather than arbitrary victims. No live runtime reproduction was executed (would require standing up the full stack + Python mesh service), so evidence stays at code+config analysis rather than CONFIRMED-RUNTIME. New evidence: MeeraSessionService.java:439-443 shows the on-behalf token is minted and returned directly to the browser in TurnResult, turning the raw-URI filter's path-matching flaw into a concretely reachable exploit rather than a purely theoretical gap.
- **Location:** influora-api/src/main/java/com/influora/security/InternalServiceTokenFilter.java:82-83; web/MeeraInternalController.java:319-372; config/SecurityConfig.java:298
- **Reproduction:** Scratchpad probe Probe.java (StrictHttpFirewall + ServletRequestPathUtils + PathPatternParser 'matches' against '/internal/meera/turns/release'), compiled against ~/.m2 spring-web 6.1.14, spring-security-web 6.3.4 and tomcat-embed-core 10.1.31.
- **Impact:** Any brand or creator can call internal Meera routes for their own workspace without the service token and HMAC. Consequences: (a) POST %69nternal/meera/turns/release after each turn refunds every AI credit, giving unlimited Meera usage on the platform's Anthropic bill; (b) POST .../messages persists attacker-authored 'assistant' turns; (c) tool routes run outside the Python tool loop's validation. Cross-tenant access is still blocked by OnBehalfAuthResolver's workspace binding.
- **Affected Features:** Meera AI credit metering, Meera turn write-back, Meera internal tools (create_campaign, request_payment, confirm_launch, show_creators, calculate_budget, get_campaign_performance, context)
- **Agents:** kabir (finder models: opus); merged from kabir:KAB-02
- **Recommended Fix:** In InternalServiceTokenFilter, match on the decoded, normalised path, as AuthRateLimitFilter does with decode() and stripMatrixParams(); or better, register it with a Spring RequestMatcher/securityMatcher for /internal/** so it shares MVC's decoding. As defence in depth, add .requestMatchers("/internal/**").hasRole("INTERNAL_SERVICE") in SecurityConfig so a user JWT can never authorise an internal route. Add a MockMvc test that sends /api/v1/%69nternal/meera/turns/release with a user JWT and asserts 401/403.
- **Verification Required:** Kavya/Meera run a MockMvc or booted-app test sending %69nternal with a brand JWT and a valid on-behalf token and observe a 200 today. Also confirm whether Caddy/nginx forward the escaped path unchanged in the live topology.

### XR-PRIYA-01 - EV-004's exact vulnerability class (raw undecoded getRequestURI() path matching) was already found and fixed in a sibling filter (AuthRateLimitFilter, attributed to Kabir) but never applied to InternalServiceTokenFilter, the highest-trust filter in the app

- **Finding:** EV-004's exact vulnerability class (raw undecoded getRequestURI() path matching) was already found and fixed in a sibling filter (AuthRateLimitFilter, attributed to Kabir) but never applied to InternalServiceTokenFilter, the highest-trust filter in the app
- **Severity:** P0 (cross-review addition by priya; not passed through the adversarial verifier)
- **Evidence Score:** E4
- **Confidence:** 88
- **Status:** OBSERVED / COMMITTED. **Still OPEN as of 2026-09-20.** EV-004's own fix (commit `be76665`) added a `RequestPaths.java` decode utility now shared by `SecurityConfig`, `AuthRateLimitFilter` and `InternalServiceTokenFilter`, which may bear on this finding's recommended fix — but this finding was not in the 2026-09-20 close list and has not itself been independently re-verified (no confirmation that an architecture test now enforces the invariant for every future filter), so it stays open pending its own review.
- **Evidence:** AuthRateLimitFilter.java lines 69-71 and 620-623 carry an explicit comment block ('[Kabir NEW-1]') stating getRequestURI() is raw/undecoded and would silently bypass literal-path matching; its bucketFor method (line 343) is `decode(stripContext(request.getRequestURI()))`. InternalServiceTokenFilter.java line 82-83 does `stripContext(request.getRequestURI())` with no decode() call anywhere in the file — the identical bug class, already named and fixed elsewhere in the same package, was never applied to this filter.
- **Location:** influora-api/src/main/java/com/influora/security/InternalServiceTokenFilter.java:82-83 (no decode) vs influora-api/src/main/java/com/influora/security/AuthRateLimitFilter.java:69-71,343,620-623 (decode() applied, with the bypass explicitly documented in comments)
- **Reproduction:** Not performed; code trace only (see Evidence).
- **Impact:** Elevates EV-004 from a single-filter bug to a systemic gap: no shared path-decoding utility or test enforces this invariant across every security filter, so the highest-trust filter regressed past a fix that already existed two files away, and any future filter can reintroduce the same class of bug undetected.
- **Affected Features:** See Impact.
- **Agents:** priya (cross-review)
- **Recommended Fix:** Extract AuthRateLimitFilter's decode()/stripContext() helpers into a shared utility used by every path-matching filter (InternalServiceTokenFilter, AuthRateLimitFilter, AnalyticsUsageCapInterceptor), and add an architecture test that fails if any filter in that package calls getRequestURI() without routing through it.
- **Verification Required:** Send a percent-encoded variant of /internal/meera/messages through the full filter chain in a Spring test slice and assert 401/403 rather than a successful controller hit.

Without printing secret values, verified length/format/prefix of the cited keys: ANTHROPIC_API_KEY 108 chars 'sk-ant-a...', GEMINI_API_KEY 35 chars 'AIzaSy...', SARVAM_API_KEY 36 chars 'sk_77nef...', R2_ACCOUNT_ID/R2_ACCESS_KEY_ID exactly 32 hex chars, R2_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY exactly 64 hex chars, MSG91_AUTH_KEY/TOKEN_AUTH 27-char alphanumeric -- all matching real provider key formats/lengths with none of the usual placeholder markers (your/xxx/changeme/replace/example/<>/***). influora-ai/env.example's own header comment claims 'NO REAL SECRETS COMMITTED HERE' directly above these live-shaped values, a self-contradiction consistent with this repo's documented pattern of stale/misleading comments. Downgraded evidence_score from the finder's 4 to 3 because I did not (and should not, per audit safety rules) call any provider API to confirm the keys are currently active -- 'still active' remains genuinely unverified, matching the original finder's own disagreement note. Committed live-format secrets across multiple historical commits still justify P0 (rotation + history purge) independent of current activity status. New evidence: influora-ai/env.example's own 'NO REAL SECRETS COMMITTED HERE' comment directly contradicts the live-format values that immediately follow it in the same file.

Kavya's review confirmed the commit touches only copy/content files plus one backend string (`EmailTemplateRegistry`) — no logic files changed, and the underlying pooled-ledger mechanism (LedgerEscrowBackend) is unchanged, as it must be: this fix corrects what is said about the mechanism, not the mechanism itself. **Explicitly still open, because code cannot close it:** legal/counsel review of who legally holds wallet balances and how insolvency is treated, whether/where TDS actually applies, the RBI-KYC citation still sitting in the privacy policy, and the stale OG image.
- **Evidence:** [tejas:TEJAS-01] LedgerEscrowBackend (the only EscrowBackend implementation) moves money between three Influora-owned Wallet database rows via an internal ledger (WalletLedgerService.post): brand wallet -> a single hardcoded 'platform-clearing-wallet' (PlatformWalletService.PLATFORM_CLEARING_WALLET_OWNER_ID) -> creator/payee wallet. The class's own javadoc states this is 'today's pooled-wallet-balance model,' explicitly incompatible with a real Razorpay Route-based per-payment-transfer implementation, which does not exist ('Building that Route implementation is explicitly out of scope here'). A repo-wide search of influora-api/src/main for 'nodal' returns zero hits.

That wallet is a single Influora-owned row (PlatformWalletService.java:24,39-43, owner 'platform-clearing-wallet'). The class is an unconditional @Service and the only EscrowBackend. The backend has no Razorpay Route or transfers call (grep for transfers/linked_account finds nothing in payment code), so captured funds settle to Influora's merchant account. User-facing copy says otherwise: src/content/legal/escrow-and-refund-policy.md:5,13 ('held... by a licensed, RBI-authorized Payment Aggregator', 'Influora never pools Campaign funds in its own bank account'), and that policy is rendered via App.tsx:787. Similar claims appear in about.tsx:96, how-it-works-brands.tsx:96,525, how-it-works-creators.tsx:89, features/deal-room.tsx:100, terms-of-service.md:24 and trust-items.ts:17. I did not refute it. I kept P0 because this is a false legal and regulatory claim about who holds customer money, and it directly breaches the standing F-0851 rule. I lowered the evidence score to 3 because I could not see a runtime reproduction, and bank-level settlement was not observed. New evidence: escrow-and-refund-policy.md:13 contains the literal 'never pools' claim and is routed at App.tsx:787. No Razorpay transfers or Route integration exists in the RC backend.

PortfolioService.declarePlatform (PortfolioService.java:448-507) lets any authenticated creator write a CreatorMetric row with dataSource=CreatorMetric.DATA_SOURCE_CREATOR_REPORTED and a self-typed followers count up to MAX_SELF_DECLARED_FOLLOWERS=1,000,000,000, with time=Instant.now() — i.e. always newer than any prior Meta-synced row. Because buildMetricsResponse orders by time only, that self-declared row becomes 'mostRecent' and is what AnalyticsContro || [blueprint-creator-onboarding-meta-analytics-portfolio:BLUEPRINT-02] PublicCreatorService.getVerifiedMetrics (influora-api/src/main/java/com/influora/service/PublicCreatorService.java:46-103) gates on profile.isDiscoverable() && metaConnected (an active, non-expired, non-revoked meta_oauth_tokens row) && !profile.isSuspended(), then reads creatorMetricsRepository.findByCreatorProfileIdOrderByTimeDesc(profile.getId(), PageRequest.of(0,1)).stream().findFirst() — the single newest CreatorMetric row for the creator, with NO filter on dataSource and NO filter on platform. The method's own comment says "the metric row IS the platform-API evidence behind this snapshot" and the frontend page this serves (/c/:username/verified) is explicitly the 'verified-metrics' surface (class javadoc: T-MEERA-CREATOR-PHASE-A). Because PortfolioService.declarePlatform (see BLUEPRINT-01) lets a Meta-connected creator ALSO self-declare a different platform (e.g. YouTube/TikTok/Twi
- **Adversarial verification:** In the RC, PortfolioService.declarePlatform (PortfolioService.java:449-500, exposed at PortfolioController.java:88) accepts INSTAGRAM/YOUTUBE/TIKTOK/TWITTER (line 134-135) and followers up to MAX_SELF_DECLARED_FOLLOWERS=1_000_000_000 (line 145). It writes a CreatorMetric with DATA_SOURCE_CREATOR_REPORTED. ## 37. P1 Findings

Twenty-one P1s: 18 register entries plus XR-KABIR-01, XR-PRIYA-02 and XR-ROHAN-01. They are ordered by the must-fix sequence: authorization before approval-release, then money-out, affiliate, platform, product wiring, and content. **Updated 2026-09-20:** EV-031 and XR-ROHAN-01 are now CLOSED (copy fixed in commit `51577f5`, part of the same six-commit wave that closed most of section 36's P0s — see their entries below and the 2026-09-20 Remediation log entry above). EV-021 and the remaining P1s are unaffected by this wave and stay open.

### EV-021 - Any workspace member (including VIEWER) can approve a deliverable, and approval releases escrow funds, bypassing the OWNER/ADMIN release gate

- **Finding:** Any workspace member (including VIEWER) can approve a deliverable, and approval releases escrow funds, bypassing the OWNER/ADMIN release gate
- **Severity:** P1
- **Evidence Score:** E3
- **Confidence:** 92 (register 85)
- **Status:** OBSERVED; verification verdict CONFIRMED / provenance COMMITTED
- **Evidence:** [kabir:KAB-03] BrandDeliverableService.approve (BrandDeliverableService.java:128-187) calls only brandContext.requireBrandWorkspace(principal) (JWT workspace claim, no membership or role) and then escrowService.tryReleaseOnApproval(workspace.getId(), deliverable.getMilestoneId()) at ~:187. The explicit EscrowService.release requires requireMember plus requireRole(OWNER, ADMIN) (EscrowService.java:593-595). MemberRole = OWNER/ADMIN/MANAGER/MEMBER/VIEWER. No test asserts a role on approve.
- **Adversarial verification:** Confirmed the full call chain for BrandDeliverableService.approve() (lines 128-187): it calls only brandContext.requireBrandWorkspace(principal) (BRAND user-type + not-deleted + workspace-not-suspended, no membership/role check) and a workspace-scoped requireBrandDeliverable lookup — neither requireMember() nor requireRole() is ever invoked. Confirmed EscrowService.tryReleaseOnApproval's own javadoc (~lines 707-716) explicitly states it runs the same release gates as release()/releaseByHoldId() 'but WITHOUT the brand OWNER/ADMIN role gate', calling this deliberate. Confirmed SecurityConfig.java has no workspace-role filter matcher for /deliverables/** (only user-TYPE matchers exist for /admin/** and /creator/**), and confirmed the FE deliverable-review-panel.tsx has no role gating either — no defense at any layer. Ran BrandDeliverableServiceTest (32/32 green); it contains no negative test for a VIEWER-role principal being rejected, consistent with the absence of the claimed gate. New evidence: Confirmed absence of a role gate at three independent layers (Spring Security config, BrandContextService, FE panel) and confirmed via the service's own javadoc that skipping the OWNER/ADMIN check in tryReleaseOnApproval was a reasoned, intentional decision rather than an oversight, which if anything strengthens the finding. Corroborates the finding's own disagreement note: the release side-effect is currently inert only because of EV-019, and the two defects mask each other's true blast radius today.
- **Location:** influora-api/src/main/java/com/influora/service/BrandDeliverableService.java:128-187; web/BrandDeliverableController.java:46-50
- **Reproduction:** POST /api/v1/brand/deliverables/{id}/approve with a VIEWER member's JWT on a SUBMITTED deliverable whose milestone is funded.
- **Impact:** A VIEWER or MEMBER seat, or a removed member within the 15-minute access-token window, can move brand escrow to a creator. That defeats the brand's own separation of duties and enables collusion between a low-privilege seat and a creator.
- **Affected Features:** Brand deliverable approval, Escrow release on approval, Workspace RBAC
- **Agents:** kabir (finder models: opus); merged from kabir:KAB-03
- **Recommended Fix:** In approve (and reject/requestRevision), call brandContext.requireMember(principal, workspace.getId()) and requireRole(member, OWNER, ADMIN[, MANAGER if product agrees]), matching the release gate. Add a test asserting VIEWER/MEMBER get 403 and that no ledger movement occurs.
- **Verification Required:** Unit test with a VIEWER member mocked on BrandContextService showing approve currently succeeds and tryReleaseOnApproval is invoked.
- **Finder disagreement noted at merge:** Release side is currently inert because tryReleaseOnApproval returns NO_MILESTONE (swapnil SWP-02); the missing role check on approve is still live and becomes a fund-release bypass the moment SWP-02 is fixed. Fix both together.

### EV-019 - Deliverable approval never releases payment (Deliverable.milestoneId never populated); release needs a second brand action and there is no auto-release

- **Finding:** Deliverable approval never releases payment (Deliverable.milestoneId never populated); release needs a second brand action and there is no auto-release
- **Severity:** P1
- **Evidence Score:** E4 (register E3)
- **Confidence:** 93 (register 90)
- **Status:** OBSERVED; verification verdict CONFIRMED-RUNTIME / provenance COMMITTED
- **Evidence:** [swapnil:SWP-02] ContractService.materializeDeliverables (ContractService.java:765-775) builds Deliverables without .milestoneId. EscrowService.tryReleaseOnApproval (EscrowService.java:729-732) returns held('NO_MILESTONE') for a null id. BrandDeliverableService.approve (BrandDeliverableService.java:191, 221-233) logs 'creator has not been paid' and returns released=false. BrandDeliverableServiceTest.java:321-323 asserts NO_MILESTONE and passes. The job/ package has no auto-release job.
- **Adversarial verification:** Statically confirmed the sole Deliverable-creation path (ContractService.java:764-772, materializeDeliverables) never calls Builder.milestoneId(...), and no other write path in influora-api/src/main ever sets deliverables.milestone_id. Confirmed EscrowService.tryReleaseOnApproval's real implementation returns ReleaseOutcome.held("NO_MILESTONE") whenever milestoneId is null/blank (BrandDeliverableService.approve() passes deliverable.getMilestoneId() directly). Executed `mvn -q -o -Dtest=BrandDeliverableServiceTest,ContractServiceTest test` in the clean RC checkout: 32/32 and 39/39 pass (0 failures/errors); BrandDeliverableServiceTest.java lines 222-234/~321-323 explicitly document and assert NO_MILESTONE for EVERY approval 'by design'. Downgrade nuance: the same RC already ships a working manual-release mitigation (F-0224, deal-payments-tab.tsx -> POST /wallet/escrow/release against a real ContractMilestone id, independent of the broken Deliverable.milestoneId link), so money is not irrecoverably stuck/refund-only as implied — but the advertised 'approve unlocks payment' flow is broken 100% of the time with no in-product prompt toward the workaround, which keeps this P1. New evidence: Confirmed a working manual-release UI/endpoint mitigation exists in the same RC (F-0224: deal-payments-tab.tsx + EscrowController.release, OWNER/ADMIN-gated, keyed off real payment_milestones ids); confirmed via green test execution that the claimed behavior is not just present but self-documented in a committed test as intentional.
- **Location:** influora-api/src/main/java/com/influora/service/ContractService.java:765-775; service/EscrowService.java:729-732; service/BrandDeliverableService.java:191-233
- **Reproduction:** Approve any deliverable on a funded deal. The ReviewResponse paymentReleased is false and the hold remains FUNDED.
- **Impact:** The creator's payday depends on the brand remembering a second 'release' click. A brand that goes silent leaves money in FUNDED, where it can then be refunded (SWP-01). This creates dispute and ops load and hurts the time-to-paid retention hook.
- **Affected Features:** Deliverable approval, Escrow release, Creator payout
- **Agents:** swapnil (finder models: opus); merged from swapnil:SWP-02
- **Recommended Fix:** Link each materialized Deliverable to its milestone (or release by collaboration's FUNDED holds on approval). Add a scheduled auto-release after N days from approval/POSTED unless disputed.
- **Verification Required:** A runtime E2E on staging confirming the hold stays FUNDED after approval.
- **Finder disagreement noted at merge:** Conflicts with blueprint-deals-contracts-content feature "Brand deliverable review ... WORKING E5" and with kabir KAB-03 which assumes approval releases escrow. The test BrandDeliverableServiceTest asserts NO_MILESTONE, i.e. the tests encode the gap.

### EV-181 - Every milestone escrow fund stalls ~50s and silently drops its application-history event, because a REQUIRES_NEW history insert deadlocks against the fund's own row lock

- **Finding:** Every milestone escrow fund stalls for roughly `innodb_lock_wait_timeout` (~50s default) and silently loses its `FUND_ESCROW` application-history row. `ApplicationHistoryService.record` runs in `REQUIRES_NEW` (`ApplicationHistoryService.java:69`) and its insert (`application_history_events.application_id` FK -> `collaborations`) waits on the same collaboration row the outer `initiateFund` transaction holds `FOR UPDATE` (`EscrowService.java` ~:525-527). A genuinely concurrent duplicate fund attempt on the same milestone then gets a raw MySQL 1205 lock-wait-timeout error, which is likely to surface to the caller as a 5xx, instead of the new clean 409 `MILESTONE_ALREADY_FUNDED` that the EV-002 fix otherwise produces. Discovered as a side effect of Meera's EV-002 concurrency probes and confirmed again at the `de249ed` commit level; pre-existing at HEAD and not introduced by the EV-002/EV-176/EV-177/EV-178 fix.
- **Severity:** P1
- **Evidence Score:** E4
- **Confidence:** 85
- **Status:** OBSERVED (Meera, real MySQL)
- **Evidence:** Meera's real-MySQL concurrency probes around the EV-002/de249ed fix: each milestone fund round took approximately 50.5s to return, and a post-run query of `application_history_events` for the funded milestones showed 0 rows after 4 successful funds, with "Could not write the application-history event" logged for each. A first probe run (before the harness reset a session-level `innodb_lock_wait_timeout` override) showed the same swallowed-history-insert stall escalate into the *losing* racer also timing out with `PessimisticLockingFailureException` instead of getting a clean 409 — though even then, only 1 hold and 1 debit were ever created, so money stayed correct.
- **Location:** influora-api/src/main/java/com/influora/service/ApplicationHistoryService.java:69 (`@Transactional(propagation = Propagation.REQUIRES_NEW)` record); service/EscrowService.java ~:525-527 (the collaboration-row `FOR UPDATE` lock held by `initiateFund`); resources/db/migration (application_history_events.application_id FK -> collaborations).
- **Reproduction:** Fund a milestone via `POST /wallet/escrow/fund` against a real MySQL 8 database (not H2/mocked) and time the call: it returns after ~50s instead of immediately, and `application_history_events` has no `FUND_ESCROW` row for that fund afterward, with a swallowed exception logged.
- **Impact:** Every real-money milestone fund is roughly 50 seconds slower than it should be and silently produces no audit trail for that fund. Under real concurrency, a losing duplicate-fund attempt can time out into a 5xx instead of a clean 409, which a client (or a user hitting retry after a slow response) can plausibly read as "try again" — a believable trigger for some of the duplicate-fund incidents this pass investigated, including possibly the one the brand owner originally reported.
- **Affected Features:** Escrow funding (milestone and campaign-pool), application/audit history, EV-002's own 409 contract under real concurrent load.
- **Agents:** meera (verify pass, finder of the runtime confirmation, both in the fix-checkout and commit-level passes for EV-002).
- **Recommended Fix:** Stop using `REQUIRES_NEW` for the history-event insert inside a transaction that already holds a row lock the insert's FK depends on. Either write the `FUND_ESCROW` history event after the outer transaction commits (a `@TransactionalEventListener(phase = AFTER_COMMIT)` listener) or fold the insert into the same transaction as the fund itself. Separately, map any resulting `PessimisticLockingFailureException`/lock-timeout to a 409/retryable response rather than letting it surface as an unhandled 5xx.
- **Verification Required:** A funding call against real MySQL should return well under 1s and should leave exactly one `FUND_ESCROW` application-history row per successful fund; a concurrent duplicate-fund race should never surface a 5xx/lock-timeout to the loser, only the 409 from EV-002's guard.

### EV-014 - Creator withdrawal idempotency key (80/82 chars from a UUID header) overflows VARCHAR(64) in wallet_transactions and payouts: every FE withdrawal fails at commit

- **Finding:** Creator withdrawal idempotency key (80/82 chars from a UUID header) overflows VARCHAR(64) in wallet_transactions and payouts: every FE withdrawal fails at commit
- **Severity:** P1
- **Evidence Score:** E4 (register E5)
- **Confidence:** 88 (register 85)
- **Status:** CONFIRMED (independent finders); verification verdict CONFIRMED / provenance COMMITTED
- **Evidence:** [meera-db:MEERA-DB-03] WalletService.java:304 scopedKey = 'creator-withdraw:'(17) + userId(26 ULID) + ':' + client key. The FE sends crypto.randomUUID() (36 chars; src/lib/api.ts:3760-3764, 4078-4079), so scopedKey is 80. WalletLedgerService.java:158/176 stores scopedKey+':D'/':C' (82) into wallet_transactions.idempotency_key VARCHAR(64) (V8:15). Payout.createQueued stores scopedKey into payouts.idempotency_key VARCHAR(64) (V48:13, Payout.java:56). The composite idempotency_keys key (wallet.withdraw:<26>:<80> = 123) passes the 128-char guard (IdempotencyService.java:80,207-209,225), so the failure happens at the ledger insert. The payout-retry path fixed the same class of bug with a SHA-256 digest (PayoutReconciliationService.java:353-427); the withdraw path was not fixed. || [vikram-pay:VIK-P1-01] FE: creator-wallet.tsx:593 uses safeRandomUUID() (crypto.randomUUID, 36 chars; fallback 'idem-...' 25 chars). BE: WalletService.java:304 scopedKey = 'creator-withdraw:' + userId(26) + ':' + key = 80 chars; passed as the ledger key (:394), where WalletLedgerService.java:159,178 appends ':D'/':C' (82 chars), and as Payout.idempotencyKey (:411). Columns: V8__wallet_transactions.sql:15 VARCHAR(64); V48__payouts.sql:13 VARCHAR(64); entity length=64 (WalletTransaction.java:61, Payout.java:56). No length validation in WalletController.java:116-125. Any client key over 18 chars overflows. MySQL 8.0 images (deploy/*/docker-compose*.yml) default to STRICT_TRANS_TABLES (acknowledged live in V20260907120000 comment line 40). WalletServiceTest is Mockito-only (@ExtendWith(MockitoExtension.class), :60) with a short constant key, so the 27 passing tests cannot see it. Mock/dev mode also overflows payou
- **Adversarial verification:** WalletService.java:304 builds scopedKey = 'creator-withdraw:' + userId + ':' + idempotencyKey. userId is a 26-char ULID (users.id VARCHAR(26); Ulids.newUlid()); idempotencyKey is a 36-char UUID minted client-side (creator-wallet.tsx:591 safeRandomUUID(), passed through unmodified, never length-checked, at WalletController.java:117-124/WalletService.java:250-254). Exact arithmetic: 80 chars for the payouts-table key, 82 chars once WalletLedgerService.java:158,176 appends ':D'/':C' for the wallet_transactions legs -- matching the finder's 80/82 precisely. V8__wallet_transactions.sql:15 and V48__payouts.sql:13 both confirm VARCHAR(64). IdempotencyService's own 128-char guard (IdempotencyService.java:80,225-235) composes a DIFFERENT, longer key against a DIFFERENT (128-char) column and does not protect this write path (123<128, passes fine). WalletServiceTest.java and WalletLedgerServiceTest.java are both pure Mockito (@Mock every repository) and structurally cannot observe a DB-level 'data too long' failure. STRICT_TRANS_TABLES is the codebase's own stated operative sql_mode with no override found, so a real insert would throw rather than truncate, and WalletLedgerService.post's DataIntegrityViolationException catch (lines 186-199) would find no existing posting and surface a 409 LEDGER_POSTING_CONFLICT -- so every real withdrawal fails, just with a misleading 409 rather than a raw 500. Did not execute a live MySQL/Testcontainers reproduction (none run locally without Docker), so stopping at CONFIRMED rather than CONFIRMED-RUNTIME. New evidence: Recomputed the exact scoped-key length (80/82 chars) from RC source and matched it to the committed VARCHAR(64) DDL. Traced the existing 128-char idempotency guard and showed it does not cover this specific write path. Confirmed both wallet tests are fully mocked.
- **Location:** influora-api/src/main/java/com/influora/service/WalletService.java:304, 381-395; service/WalletLedgerService.java:158,176; V8__wallet_transactions.sql:15; V48__payouts.sql:13 | influora-api/src/main/java/com/influora/service/WalletService.java:304,385-412; src/pages/creator-wallet.tsx:593; db/migration/V8__wallet_transactions.sql:15; V48__payouts.sql:13
- **Reproduction:** Arithmetic from source: 17+26+1+36 = 80; +2 = 82 > 64. On MySQL 8 strict, calling POST /wallet/withdraw as a KYC'd creator with a primary bank account and Idempotency-Key = a UUID fails.
- **Impact:** Every creator withdrawal (POST /wallet/withdraw) with a key longer than 18 chars fails with 'Data too long' and returns 409/500. The creator cannot withdraw. No money moves, because the tx rolls back.
- **Affected Features:** Creator withdrawal (RazorpayX payout rail), Creator withdrawal (POST /wallet/withdraw), Creator payout history (GET /wallet/payouts)
- **Agents:** meera-db, vikram-pay (finder models: opus); merged from meera-db:MEERA-DB-03, vikram-pay:VIK-P1-01
- **Recommended Fix:** Hash the client key the same way as retryDebitKey: scopedKey = 'creator-withdraw:' + sha256(userId + ':' + key).substring(0,32), which gives 49 chars (+2 for the ledger suffix = 51). Or widen both columns to VARCHAR(128) via a migration. Add a test asserting generated key length <= column length for every ledger caller.
- **Verification Required:** Execute on staging/Testcontainers MySQL with PAYOUTS_ENABLED on. Confirm whether prod currently exposes withdraw (src/lib/api.ts:121 gate).

### EV-003 - Creator withdrawal calls RazorpayX inside the open DB transaction (wallet + clearing-wallet row locks held) before any durable Payout row, and marks idempotency COMPLETED before commit

- **Finding:** Creator withdrawal calls RazorpayX inside the open DB transaction (wallet + clearing-wallet row locks held) before any durable Payout row, and marks idempotency COMPLETED before commit
- **Severity:** P1 (register P0, downgraded by verifier)
- **Evidence Score:** E4 (register E5)
- **Confidence:** 75 (register 72)
- **Status:** CONFIRMED (independent finders); verification verdict DOWNGRADED / provenance COMMITTED
- **Evidence:** [vikram-pay:VIK-P0-01] WalletService.requestCreatorWithdrawal is public @Transactional (WalletService.java:249-250) and takes PESSIMISTIC_WRITE on the creator wallet (:263-265, WalletRepository.java:17-19). Inside it, executeOnce runs doProcessWithdrawal (its own @Transactional at :381 is inert: protected + self-invoked via lambda at :311), which calls ledgerService.post (joins the ambient tx, locks the platform clearing wallet row FOR UPDATE, WalletLedgerService.java:102-103), then razorpayXClient.initiatePayout (:398-399, real HTTP), then payoutRepository.save (:401-412). IdempotencyService.executeOnce marks the key COMPLETED in REQUIRES_NEW right after action.get() returns (IdempotencyService.java:157-159; IdempotencyReservationOps.java:144-148), i.e. BEFORE the ambient commit. RazorpayXClient builds the request with no .timeout() (RazorpayXClient.java:138-145; only connectTimeout at :86). Contrast PayoutSe || [swapnil:SWP-08] WalletService.requestCreatorWithdrawal is @Transactional (WalletService.java:249-250) and holds findByOwnerIdForUpdate. doProcessWithdrawal (381-410) posts the debit, calls razorpayXClient.initiatePayout, and only then saves the Payout. PayoutService, by contrast, persists a PENDING Payout before the gateway call so that PayoutOrphanedDebitSweepJob can reconcile it. If the process dies after RazorpayX accepts but before commit, the debit rolls back while the money has left, and no Payout row exists for the sweep to find. No test covers a gateway failure or crash after the debit (WalletServiceTest methods listed at lines 311-622).
- **Adversarial verification:** Confirmed the mechanism is real: doProcessWithdrawal's own @Transactional is a Spring self-invocation no-op (called via `this.` inside requestCreatorWithdrawal's already-open transaction), so the ledger debit, RazorpayX call, and Payout row all share one physical transaction; IdempotencyService.executeOnce (156-164, exact match) calls reservationOps.markCompleted immediately after doProcessWithdrawal returns successfully, and markCompleted runs in its own genuine @Transactional(REQUIRES_NEW) bean (IdempotencyReservationOps), so it commits independently and BEFORE the ambient withdrawal transaction's own commit. I found a concrete, non-hypothetical trigger for a late commit-time failure that the original finder did not cite: payouts.idempotency_key is VARCHAR(64) (V48__payouts.sql:13), and doProcessWithdrawal's stored key (44 fixed bytes of prefix/userId plus the client's Idempotency-Key, which creator-wallet.tsx defaults to a 36-char UUID) reliably overflows it -- 80 > 64 -- and unlike the parallel case the team already hardened (IdempotencyReservationOps.tryReserve uses saveAndFlush specifically to avoid this class of deferred-flush bug), doProcessWithdrawal's payoutRepository.save() is a plain save(), leaving the same hazard live. However, I downgrade severity from the claimed P0 because this path is not reachable in production today: src/lib/api.ts confirms VITE_PAYOUTS_ENABLED defaults to disabled (money-OUT deliberately stays off, unlike money-IN which defaults on), creator-wallet.tsx's withdraw button is disabled while blocked, and application.yml's razorpay.payout-account-number defaults to the literal placeholder REPLACE_WITH_YOUR_RAZORPAYX_ACCOUNT. This matches swapnil's gated/INFERRED framing more than vikram-pay's P0 -- it is a real, well-evidenced go-live blocker for turning withdrawals on, not a currently-live P0 incident. New evidence: Computed that payouts.idempotency_key (VARCHAR(64), V48__payouts.sql:13) is reliably overflowed by doProcessWithdrawal's stored scoped key given creator-wallet.tsx's default UUID-based Idempotency-Key (44 fixed bytes + 36-char UUID = 80 > 64) -- a concrete trigger, not just a hypothesis. Confirmed doProcessWithdrawal's payoutRepository.save() lacks the saveAndFlush() hardening the team already applied to the structurally identical hazard in IdempotencyReservationOps.tryReserve. Confirmed VITE_PAYOUTS_ENABLED defaults false and the RazorpayX account number defaults to an unconfigured placeholder, so the path is currently flag-gated off in production.
- **Location:** influora-api/src/main/java/com/influora/service/WalletService.java:249-323,381-415; IdempotencyService.java:156-164; RazorpayXClient.java:117-155 | influora-api/src/main/java/com/influora/service/WalletService.java:249-322, 381-410
- **Reproduction:** Static: follow the call chain above. Runtime repro requires MySQL + a RazorpayX sandbox that accepts the payout; not performed.
- **Impact:** Any commit-time failure after RazorpayX accepts the payout (such as the deterministic key-length failure in VIK-P1-01, a deadlock, or a connection drop) rolls back the ledger debit and the payout row while the bank transfer stands. There is then no payouts row for the webhook to reconcile against ('No Payout row found... acknowledged', PayoutReconciliationService.java:123-131). The key is COMPLETED, so a retry returns 409 forever, and the FE mints a fresh key on the next dialog, so the same balance can be withdrawn again. Separately, the platform clearing-wallet row lock is held for the whole unbounded RazorpayX HTTP call, which stalls every top-up credit, escrow fund/release, fee and payout platform-wide.
- **Affected Features:** Creator withdrawal (POST /wallet/withdraw), All ledger movements touching the clearing wallet (availability), Creator withdrawal (RazorpayX)
- **Agents:** swapnil, vikram-pay (finder models: opus); merged from vikram-pay:VIK-P0-01, swapnil:SWP-08
- **Recommended Fix:** Restructure withdrawal like PayoutService: remove @Transactional from requestCreatorWithdrawal; commit a PENDING Payout row plus the ledger debit in one short transaction (through a separate bean so it is really transactional); call RazorpayX outside any transaction; then record the gateway result in a second transaction. Mark the idempotency row COMPLETED only after that commit. Extend PayoutOrphanedDebitSweepJob to cover withdrawal rows (milestoneId null). Add HttpRequest.timeout() to every RazorpayX/Razorpay call.
- **Verification Required:** Integration test on MySQL (Testcontainers) that forces a commit failure after a stubbed successful initiatePayout and asserts a durable PENDING/QUEUED payouts row and debit survive. Also a lock-hold test showing no clearing-wallet lock is held during the gateway call.
- **Recorded disagreement:** Evidence disputed: verifier E4/C75 vs Priya E3/C80 (static only; trigger never reproduced).

### EV-020 - Creator bank payout is not self-serve in the production build: withdraw is off by default and payouts are manual admin UTR entries

- **Finding:** Creator bank payout is not self-serve in the production build: withdraw is off by default and payouts are manual admin UTR entries
- **Severity:** P1
- **Evidence Score:** E3
- **Confidence:** 90 (register 88)
- **Status:** OBSERVED; verification verdict CONFIRMED / provenance CONFIGURATION
- **Evidence:** [swapnil:SWP-03] Dockerfile:58 ARG VITE_PAYOUTS_ENABLED=false. .env.production:73 VITE_PAYOUTS_ENABLED=false. src/lib/api.ts:109 and :119-122 isMoneyActionBlocked('withdraw'). src/pages/creator-wallet.tsx:683-697 disables Withdraw and shows 'Bank transfers are being switched on'. The only production money-out path is AdminFinanceService.recordManualPayout (AdminFinanceService.java:150-272; SUPER_ADMIN plus MFA, UTR required, debits the creator wallet). Also, RazorpayXClient.isConfigured (RazorpayXClient.java:108-110) only checks the payout account is non-blank, and application.yml:404 defaults it to the non-blank placeholder 'REPLACE_WITH_YOUR_RAZORPAYX_ACCOUNT' [REDACTED-style placeholder, not a secret]. SecretsStartupValidator checks the Razorpay key/secret/webhook placeholders but not the RazorpayX account number.
- **Adversarial verification:** Confirmed VITE_PAYOUTS_ENABLED=false in Dockerfile (~line 58) and .env.production (line 73), documented as deliberate 'collection live, payouts manual' state, and confirmed this is a frontend-only Vite build flag with zero backend enforcement — WalletService.requestCreatorWithdrawal (POST /wallet/withdraw) is live server-side with no equivalent gate. Confirmed AdminFinanceService.recordManualPayout (~line 160, within cited 150-272 range) requires AdminRole.SUPER_ADMIN specifically (single-role, MFA-satisfied), distinct from the SUPER_ADMIN+ADMIN pair used nearby for read-only access. Confirmed RazorpayXClient.isConfigured() (lines 108-110) only checks non-blank values with no placeholder detection, and confirmed deploy/utho/generate-env.sh literally ships RAZORPAY_KEY_ID=rzp_test_REPLACE_WITH_YOUR_KEY / RAZORPAY_KEY_SECRET=REPLACE_WITH_YOUR_RAZORPAY_SECRET / RAZORPAYX_ACCOUNT_NUMBER=REPLACE_ME — all non-blank, so isConfigured() deterministically evaluates true against these real deployed defaults. New evidence: Confirmed the backend money-out path (POST /wallet/withdraw) has no server-side flag mirroring VITE_PAYOUTS_ENABLED, so the 'off by default' protection is UI-only; confirmed wallet debit happens before the RazorpayX call (WalletService.java ~201-205) and that PayoutOrphanedDebitSweepJob exists specifically to sweep the resulting orphaned-debit class. Ran existing RazorpayXClientTest (11/11 green) but it only covers the blank-string case, not the actually-deployed REPLACE_ME/REPLACE_WITH_YOUR_KEY placeholder strings — a genuine test-coverage gap, kept evidence_score at 3 (not 4) since I did not add a new runtime probe given the read-only-on-source constraint.
- **Location:** Dockerfile:58; .env.production:73; src/lib/api.ts:109-122; src/pages/creator-wallet.tsx:683-697; influora-api/src/main/java/com/influora/service/admin/AdminFinanceService.java:150-272; integration/razorpay/RazorpayXClient.java:108-110
- **Reproduction:** Build the FE with default args, log in as a creator, open /creator/wallet: Withdraw is disabled.
- **Impact:** Every creator payout needs a SUPER_ADMIN to do a manual bank transfer and enter it. The cost grows linearly with volume and payout delays hurt creator retention. If the flag is flipped without a real RazorpayX account, withdrawals fail at the gateway.
- **Affected Features:** Creator withdrawal, Creator payout, Admin finance
- **Agents:** swapnil (finder models: opus); merged from swapnil:SWP-03
- **Recommended Fix:** Decide the launch payout mode explicitly. If manual, give admins a queue of creators with withdrawable balance and a payout-request button for creators. If RazorpayX, add the payout account number to SecretsStartupValidator's placeholder checks before enabling VITE_PAYOUTS_ENABLED.
- **Verification Required:** Check the GitHub repo variable PAYOUTS_ENABLED and the live bundle's flag value.

- **Finding:** wallet_transactions ENUMs never widened for AFFILIATE_COMMISSION / AFFILIATE_EARNING: every affiliate commission ledger posting fails in MySQL
- **Severity:** P1
- **Evidence Score:** E4 (register E5)

No later migration ALTERs wallet_transactions. WalletTransactionType.AFFILIATE_COMMISSION and TxnReferenceType.AFFILIATE_EARNING were added by F-0402. AffiliateSettlementWriter.creditCreatorWallet:132-141 posts exactly those values through WalletLedgerService.post, which saves two WalletTransaction rows (WalletLedgerService.java:146-176). @Enumerated(STRING) with no columnDefinition. H2 @DataJpaTest (create-drop) and Mockito tests cannot see this. || [vikram-pay:VIK-P1-04] WalletTransactionType.java adds AFFILIATE_COMMISSION; TxnReferenceType.java adds AFFILIATE_EARNING. wallet_transactions.type/reference_type are MySQL ENUMs without them (V8__wallet_transactions.sql:5-6,11), and no later migration alters wallet_transactions (grep across db/migration). WalletTransaction maps them @Enumerated(STRING) (WalletTransaction.java:30-51). AffiliateSettlementWriter.creditCreatorWallet posts them (job/AffiliateSettlementWriter.java:132-142). The only tests are Mockito (CreatorAffiliateEarningSettlementTest:59), and they pass.
- **Adversarial verification:** V8__wallet_transactions.sql:6-7,12 defines native MySQL ENUMs for type/reference_type that do NOT include AFFILIATE_COMMISSION/AFFILIATE_EARNING; grepped all ~140 migrations for any ALTER touching wallet_transactions -- none exists. WalletTransactionType.java/TxnReferenceType.java DO have these values (added under [F-0402]); WalletTransaction entity maps both with @Enumerated(EnumType.STRING) against MySQLDialect + ddl-auto:validate. AffiliateSettlementWriter.creditCreatorWallet:130-142 posts exactly these values inside doSettleCreator's @Transactional boundary, so a strict-mode rejection rolls back the whole batch. Ran `mvn -q -o -Dtest=AffiliateSettlementJobTest,AffiliateSettlementWriterNullCollaboratorLogTest test`: exit 0, 11/11 green -- both classes are pure Mockito (@ExtendWith(MockitoExtension.class), every repo @Mock), incapable of catching a real-DB ENUM rejection. The repo's one Testcontainers/MySQL test in this area (DatabaseConstraintIntegrationTest) only covers the affiliate_earnings UNIQUE constraint, not this insert path. msa's 'WORKING E4' rating rests on tests structurally unable to see this. New evidence: Executed the two cited unit test classes against the RC via mvn -Dtest=...: BUILD SUCCESS, 11/11 passed, empirically confirming the tests cannot detect the DB-level mismatch; confirmed via full-migration grep that no ALTER ever widened the enum.
- **Location:** influora-api/src/main/resources/db/migration/V8__wallet_transactions.sql:6-12; influora-api/src/main/java/com/influora/job/AffiliateSettlementWriter.java:132-141; domain/enums/WalletTransactionType.java; domain/enums/TxnReferenceType.java | influora-api/src/main/java/com/influora/job/AffiliateSettlementWriter.java:132-142; db/migration/V8__wallet_transactions.sql:5-11
- **Reproduction:** On MySQL 8 with default sql_mode: INSERT INTO wallet_transactions (..., type, ...) VALUES (..., 'AFFILIATE_COMMISSION', ...) fails with error 1265. In-app: create a PENDING affiliate_earnings row older than the settlement window and trigger AffiliateSettlementJob.
- **Impact:** The monthly AffiliateSettlementJob (cron 0 0 5 1 * *, AffiliateSettlementJob.java:131) fails with 'Data truncated for column type' under STRICT_TRANS_TABLES. doSettleCreator rolls back, earnings stay PENDING, and the idempotency reservation is marked FAILED and retried every month without ever succeeding. Creator affiliate commission is never paid. No double pay.
- **Affected Features:** Affiliate commission settlement, Creator wallet credit for coupon conversions, Creator affiliate earnings page
- **Agents:** meera-db, vikram-pay (finder models: opus); merged from meera-db:MEERA-DB-01, vikram-pay:VIK-P1-04
- **Recommended Fix:** Add a new migration: ALTER TABLE wallet_transactions MODIFY type ENUM(...existing..., 'AFFILIATE_COMMISSION') NOT NULL, MODIFY reference_type ENUM(...existing..., 'AFFILIATE_EARNING') NULL. Add a conformance test asserting each Java enum value is in the corresponding DB ENUM, parsed from the migration chain.
