### EV-025 - Affiliate commission credited to a wallet owned by creator_profiles.id, not the creator's users.id

- **Finding:** Affiliate commission credited to a wallet owned by creator_profiles.id, not the creator's users.id
- **Severity:** P1
- **Evidence Score:** E3
- **Confidence:** 85 (register 80)
- **Status:** OBSERVED; verification verdict CONFIRMED / provenance COMMITTED
- **Evidence:** [vikram-pay:VIK-P1-05] affiliate_earnings.creator_id is FK creator_profiles(id) (V28__affiliate_earnings_settlement.sql:53,74), copied from coupon_codes.creator_id, also FK creator_profiles (V24__coupon_codes.sql:29,47; AffiliateEarningsService.java doRecordEarning .creatorId(coupon.getCreatorId())). AffiliateSettlementWriter.java:130 calls walletService.requireOrCreateUserWallet(earning.getCreatorId()), which creates or looks up a wallet by ownerId=profileId (WalletService.java:693-698). Creator reads use principal.getUserId() (WalletService.getBalanceForUser/getSummaryForUser, :129-138,208-226), and the profile id is a distinct ULID (AuthService.java:427-428).
- **Adversarial verification:** Traced the full ID chain in rc-115f698 (all files clean, matches HEAD 115f698). AffiliateSettlementWriter.java:130-142 credits earning.getCreatorId() via walletService.requireOrCreateUserWallet(...), which looks up/creates a Wallet keyed by ownerId=userId (WalletService.java:694-698; every other WalletService method — getBalanceForUser, getSummaryForUser, requestCreatorWithdrawal — is keyed the same way on users.id from the JWT). But AffiliateEarning.creatorId is set from CouponCode.getCreatorId() (AffiliateEarningsService.java:492), and CouponCodeService.buildAndSaveCoupon sets CouponCode.creatorId = creator.getId() where `creator` is a CreatorProfile resolved via creatorProfileRepository.findById(...) (CouponCodeService.java:189-219) — i.e. creator_profiles.id, NOT users.id. CreatorProfile has a distinct PK `id` plus a separate `user_id` FK column (CreatorProfile.java:22-27), confirming these are different values. AffiliateEarningsService.listForCreator itself queries by profile.getId() (a CreatorProfile id), corroborating that AffiliateEarning.creatorId is profile-scoped. By contrast, the sibling PayoutService.java:367 passes collaboration.getCreatorId() into the same requireOrCreateUserWallet, and Collaboration.creatorId is populated from `creatorUserId` (Collaboration.java:115/140/336) — a genuine users.id — so this is a real, isolated type mismatch specific to the affiliate-settlement path, not a systemic convention. The regression test CreatorAffiliateEarningSettlementTest mocks WalletService entirely (classic 'mock stubs the layer where the bug lives'), so it cannot and does not catch the ID-type mismatch — it only proves a credit call happens, not that it targets the right wallet. AffiliateSettlementJob is a live @Scheduled(cron) job (AffiliateSettlementJob.java:131), so this path is reachable in production once earnings exist. Not runtime-executed (no test isolates the ID mismatch), so evidence_score stays at 3 rather than 4. Note: the finder's own caveat ('once EV-011 is fixed') means I cannot independently confirm this path is currently exercised end-to-end without EV-011's content, which was not provided to me. New evidence: Cross-file confirmation that CreatorProfile.id != CreatorProfile.userId, and a same-shaped call site (PayoutService->Collaboration) that correctly uses a genuine userId, proving the affiliate path's mismatch is a real, isolated defect rather than an intentional/systemic convention.
- **Location:** influora-api/src/main/java/com/influora/job/AffiliateSettlementWriter.java:129-142
- **Reproduction:** Static id-domain trace.
- **Impact:** Once VIK-P1-04 is fixed, every commission lands in an orphan USER wallet the creator can never see or withdraw. The money is stranded and the creator dashboard shows nothing.
- **Affected Features:** Affiliate commission settlement, Creator wallet balance
- **Agents:** vikram-pay (finder models: opus); merged from vikram-pay:VIK-P1-05
- **Recommended Fix:** Resolve creatorProfileRepository.findById(earning.getCreatorId()).getUserId() before requireOrCreateUserWallet. Backfill or merge any orphan wallets keyed by profile ids.
- **Verification Required:** Integration test: settle an earning, then GET /wallet/balance as that creator and see the commission.

### EV-033 - Affiliate commission is funded from the platform clearing wallet with no brand-side debit

- **Finding:** Affiliate commission is funded from the platform clearing wallet with no brand-side debit
- **Severity:** P1
- **Evidence Score:** E3 (register E2)
- **Confidence:** 82 (register 70)
- **Status:** OBSERVED; verification verdict CONFIRMED / provenance COMMITTED
- **Evidence:** [vikram-pay:VIK-P1-06] AffiliateSettlementWriter.java:131-142 posts clearing wallet (debit) to creator wallet (credit). AffiliateSettlementJob.java has no workspace/brand wallet reference (grep 'workspace|brand|post(' returns only javadoc). The clearing wallet is exempt from the non-negative check (WalletLedgerService.java:131-139), so the debit always succeeds and goes negative.
- **Adversarial verification:** AffiliateSettlementWriter.creditCreatorWallet() (114-143) posts exactly one ledger leg: debit platformWalletService.requireClearingWallet(), credit the creator wallet, for earning.getCommissionAmount() -- no compensating brand-side debit anywhere. Grepped all 20 RC files referencing AffiliateEarning for ledger/debit/BrandWallet calls: zero hits outside this one writer. The only other 'commissionAmount' reference, CommissionInvoiceService.java, was opened and confirmed unrelated (platform take-rate/GST invoicing wired to BrandCampaignFeeService/PlatformFeeService, not the affiliate program). Confirmed WalletLedgerService.java:131-139 exactly: the clearing wallet is explicitly exempted from the non-negative-balance check, so this debit can push it arbitrarily negative unchecked. The exemption's own comment (120-130) documents a different rationale (brand top-up webhook timing lag), not affiliate funding, suggesting its use here is an undocumented side effect rather than a designed funding path. No job monitors clearing-wallet balance for this case specifically. This is a structurally confirmed, silent, uncapped platform liability leak. New evidence: The clearing-wallet negative-balance exemption's own comment describes a different, bounded scenario (brand top-up timing lag) with no mention of affiliate commissions -- its use in AffiliateSettlementWriter looks like an undocumented side effect, not a deliberate design. Distinct from the existing project memory note describing affiliate commission as 'never credited' (opposite failure mode); the two audits appear to be looking at different states/paths of the affiliate settlement flow and should be reconciled.
- **Location:** influora-api/src/main/java/com/influora/job/AffiliateSettlementWriter.java:129-142; WalletLedgerService.java:131-139
- **Reproduction:** Static.
- **Impact:** Commission is paid out of the pooled cash that backs brand deposits and escrow. The clearing account stops reconciling to the Razorpay/bank balance, and the platform silently absorbs every commission. Latent today only because VIK-P1-04/05 block settlement.
- **Affected Features:** Affiliate commission, Ledger reconciliation
- **Agents:** vikram-pay (finder models: opus); merged from vikram-pay:VIK-P1-06
- **Recommended Fix:** Debit the brand workspace wallet (or a dedicated brand-funded commission pool) at earning or settlement time, with an insufficient-balance policy, before crediting the creator.
- **Verification Required:** After settlement, clearing wallet delta = 0 and brand wallet delta = -commission.

### XR-KABIR-01 - No escrow money-movement (fund/release/refund) records which authenticated workspace member performed it — no actor column on escrow_holds/wallet_transactions, and the one ApplicationHistoryEvent write hardcodes actorId="system"

- **Finding:** No escrow money-movement (fund/release/refund) records which authenticated workspace member performed it — no actor column on escrow_holds/wallet_transactions, and the one ApplicationHistoryEvent write hardcodes actorId="system"

- **Confidence:** 82
- **Status:** OBSERVED / COMMITTED
- **Evidence:** grep -n "applicationHistoryService.record|\\.record(" EscrowService.java returns exactly ONE match (line 554), whose actorType/actorId arguments are ApplicationHistoryActorType.SYSTEM and the string literal "system" — not principal-derived — despite being reached from initiateFund, which has an authenticated AuthPrincipal in scope (used only for authorization, then discarded). grep -n "actorId|performedBy|initiatedBy|memberId" over the same file returns zero matches. V9__escrow_holds.sql and V8__wallet_transactions.sql both carry workspace_id/wallet_id but no member/actor/user/initiated_by column. EscrowController.java has zero log.* calls anywhere.
- **Location:** influora-api/src/main/resources/db/migration/V9__escrow_holds.sql:1-26 (no actor/member column); V8__wallet_transactions.sql:1-20 (same); influora-api/src/main/java/com/influora/service/EscrowService.java:212-341 (initiateFund receives AuthPrincipal principal but never threads it into applyFunding, ApplicationHistoryEvent, or any log call), :473-517 with the sole record() call at :551-566 (hardcodes ApplicationHistoryActorType.SYSTEM / literal "system" even though reachable from the brand-authenticated initiateFund path), :959-1015 refundInternal (zero application-history/log calls), release path ~:893-955 (same); influora-api/src/main/java/com/influora/web/EscrowController.java (zero log.* statements across all five money-moving endpoints).
- **Reproduction:** Not performed; code trace only (see Evidence).
- **Impact:** Combined with EV-021 (a VIEWER can approve a deliverable and trigger a real escrow release) and EV-015 (an OWNER/ADMIN can refund a FUNDED hold even after the creator's work was delivered and approved), the DB, the append-only application-history timeline, and the application logs are all silent on WHICH specific member/session of a multi-seat brand workspace funded, released, or refunded a given hold — only the workspace as a whole is recorded. A compromised/offboarded seat (see EV-071/EV-129 on token survival) leaves no durable record to answer 'who authorized this transfer' for a payment dispute, chargeback defense, or fraud investigation.
- **Affected Features:** Escrow fund (POST /wallet/escrow/fund), Escrow release (POST /wallet/escrow/release), Escrow refund (POST /wallet/escrow/refund), Payment dispute / chargeback defense, Multi-seat workspace accountability and fraud investigation
- **Agents:** kabir (cross-review)
- **Recommended Fix:** Add an actor_user_id/actor_member_id column to escrow_holds and wallet_transactions (or a dedicated escrow_hold_events audit table); thread principal.getUserId()/member.getId() from EscrowController through initiateFund/release/refund into that column and into an ApplicationHistoryEvent.record() call for all three actions (today only fund gets one, hardcoded to SYSTEM). Reserve SYSTEM for the genuinely-unauthenticated confirmFunded webhook path.
- **Verification Required:** Fund, then release (or refund) an escrow hold as two differently-named workspace members on staging and confirm none of escrow_holds, wallet_transactions, application_history_events, or API logs identify which member performed each action.

### EV-036 - AdminCreatorAgentController#setMonthlyCap performs no explicit role-tier or MFA check, unlike every other financially-relevant admin write in this domain

The codebase's own established convention for sensitive admin mutations, AdminContextService.requireRoleWithMfaSatisfied (whose javadoc says every privileged mutating endpoint should call it), is used across most other Admin*Service classes but never by this one (confirmed via grep). The endpoint's own unit test comment concedes the gate is purely structural/blanket-ADMIN. New evidence: Grep confirmed CreatorAgentPreferencesService/AdminCreatorAgentController never call AdminContextService, unlike AdminBillingService/AdminFinanceService/etc.; full trace of UserType vs AdminRole confirms any admin tier including MFA-exempt SUPPORT can reach this endpoint.

- **Impact:** If CreatorAgentPreferencesService.adminSetMonthlyCapOverride (not read this pass) also performs no role-tier check, then ANY authenticated admin JWT of ANY tier (including a hypothetical low-privilege SUPPORT-tier admin) — with no MFA requirement — could set or clear another creator's monthly AI-spend cap, a financially-relevant control that every comparable write elsewhere in this domain (billing comp/override, fee-config, manual payout, budget override) restricts to SUPER_ADMIN with MFA.

- **Agents:** blueprint-admin-moderation-ops (finder models: sonnet); merged from blueprint-admin-moderation-ops:ADM-02
- **Recommended Fix:** Read CreatorAgentPreferencesService.adminSetMonthlyCapOverride's full body to confirm whether the role/MFA gate lives there instead (not verified in this pass — the controller itself visibly does not call it). If it does not, add an explicit AdminContextService.requireRoleWithMfaSatisfied call before the write, matching the tier used for other financially-relevant admin actions in this domain.

### EV-022 - Admin MFA secret can be overwritten while MFA is enabled (no current-code or password re-verification)

- **Finding:** Admin MFA secret can be overwritten while MFA is enabled (no current-code or password re-verification)
- **Severity:** P1
- **Evidence Score:** E3
- **Confidence:** 85
- **Status:** OBSERVED; verification verdict CONFIRMED / provenance COMMITTED
- **Evidence:** [kabir:KAB-04] AdminAuthService.setupMfa (AdminAuthService.java:278-291) always runs admin.stageMfaSecret(mfaSecretCipher.encrypt(secret)), which sets encryptedMfaSecret unconditionally (AdminUser.java:242-245), and returns the plaintext secret. There is no isMfaEnabled() guard and no current TOTP or password check. login() verifies TOTP against getEncryptedMfaSecret() (AdminAuthService.java:152), so the new secret takes effect immediately. The route is POST /admin/auth/mfa/setup (AdminAuthController.java:114-118), reachable with any admin access token. Admin tokens are stored in localStorage (src/admin/services/admin-session.ts:107). No test covers setupMfa.
- **Adversarial verification:** AdminAuthController.java:114-118 gates POST /admin/auth/mfa/setup with only requireAdminId (bare valid JWT), not requireRoleWithMfaSatisfied. AdminAuthService.java:277-291 (setupMfa) unconditionally generates a new TOTP secret and calls admin.stageMfaSecret(...) regardless of admin.isMfaEnabled(). AdminUser.java:242-251 (stageMfaSecret/confirmMfa) enforce nothing about prior MFA state by design (entity is a 'plain data holder' per its own javadoc). No password re-check, no current-TOTP challenge required to re-enroll. No test in influora-api/src/test covers setupMfa at all. No mitigating control found anywhere in the call path. New evidence: Traced the full call chain (controller -> service -> entity) and confirmed the ONLY MFA gate in the codebase (AdminContextService.requireMfaSatisfied) rejects calls when MFA is NOT enabled (403 MFA_SETUP_REQUIRED) but has zero effect once MFA IS enabled, and setupMfa/verifyMfa never call it anyway.
- **Location:** influora-api/src/main/java/com/influora/service/admin/AdminAuthService.java:278-291; domain/entity/AdminUser.java:242
- **Reproduction:** With an enrolled admin's access token: POST /api/v1/admin/auth/mfa/setup; enrol the returned secret; POST /mfa/verify; the original authenticator no longer works.
- **Impact:** A stolen or XSS'd 15-minute admin access token becomes a durable second factor bound to the attacker's authenticator. Even the setup call alone invalidates the real admin's authenticator, so they cannot log in until a SUPER_ADMIN resets them.
- **Affected Features:** Admin login / MFA, Admin panel
- **Agents:** kabir (finder models: opus); merged from kabir:KAB-04
- **Recommended Fix:** Reject setupMfa when mfaEnabled is true (require the SUPER_ADMIN reset path), or require a valid code from the CURRENT secret plus the password. Stage the new secret in a separate pending column and promote it only in verifyMfa. Audit-log MFA changes and notify the admin by email.
- **Verification Required:** Unit test: an enrolled admin calls setupMfa and login with the old TOTP now fails (demonstrates the defect); after the fix, setupMfa returns 409.

### EV-017 - influora-api Redis host never wired in any production compose (defaults to localhost:6379); @Cacheable paths fail, and the CI Redis env var uses Boot-2 names so CI cannot catch it

- **Finding:** influora-api Redis host never wired in any production compose (defaults to localhost:6379); @Cacheable paths fail, and the CI Redis env var uses Boot-2 names so CI cannot catch it
- **Severity:** P1
- **Evidence Score:** E3 (register E5)
- **Confidence:** 75 (register 78)
- **Status:** CONFIRMED (independent finders); verification verdict CONFIRMED / provenance CONFIGURATION
- **Evidence:** [priya:PRI-01] pom.xml:64-65 spring-boot-starter-data-redis; config/RedisCacheConfig.java @EnableCaching (no CacheErrorHandler anywhere in src/main); no spring.data.redis.* in application.yml/-prod/-dev; influora-api environment: in deploy/utho/docker-compose.utho.yml, docker-compose.utho-shared.yml and deploy/hostinger/docker-compose.hostinger.yml has no SPRING_DATA_REDIS_HOST (only deploy/hostinger/docker-compose.test.yml:89-90 sets it); the api service depends_on redis. The repo's own test influora-api/src/test/java/com/influora/service/EscrowReleaseGateIntegrationTest.java:75-87 states that without Redis the first @Cacheable call throws RedisConnectionFailureException, and masks it with spring.cache.type=none. @Cacheable callers: HsnSacCodeService.resolveCode (used by billing/InvoiceService:258 and CampaignServiceInvoiceService), AdminDashboardStatsCache:71, CreatorDiscoveryFacetsCache:61. || [perf:PERF-03] Spring Boot 3.3.5 (influora-api/pom.xml:8-11) binds Redis host via `spring.data.redis.host` / env `SPRING_DATA_REDIS_HOST`, defaulting to `localhost`. deploy/hostinger/docker-compose.hostinger.yml's influora-api service environment block (grepped in full) sets no SPRING_DATA_REDIS_HOST/PORT despite the file also defining a separate `redis` container reachable only at hostname `redis`. The sibling deploy/hostinger/docker-compose.test.yml:88-89 explicitly sets `SPRING_DATA_REDIS_HOST: redis` with the comment 'Point Spring at the redis container (default is localhost -> Connection refused)'. Neither application.yml nor application-prod.yml sets any spring.data.redis.* property either (grepped, zero matches). AdminDashboardStatsCache.pulseStats() is `@Cacheable(cacheNames = RedisCacheConfig.ADMIN_PULSE_CACHE, ...)` and is called from AdminDashboardService.java:103 (`return statsCache.pulseSt || [perf:PERF-04] .github/workflows/backend-tests.yml:74-75 sets `SPRING_REDIS_HOST: 127.0.0.1` / `SPRING_REDIS_PORT: 6379`. In Spring Boot 3.x (confirmed 3.3.5 via pom.xml), the property was renamed to `spring.data.redis.host`/`port`; `spring.redis.*` is not bound to anything, so these two env vars have no effect on the running application. The tests still pass because a GitHub Actions service container exposes Redis on the runner's own `127.0.0.1`, which happens to match Spring Data Redis's own default host -- so CI is 'accidentally green' regardless of whether the env var binding works, and would not have caught the production docker-compose gap in PERF-03 even if it existed at the time these tests ran.
- **Adversarial verification:** Verified in rc-115f698: influora-api's service block in all three prod compose files (hostinger, utho, utho-shared) only has depends_on:redis, no SPRING_DATA_REDIS_HOST/PORT; REDIS_URL is wired only to the separate influora-ai (Python) service. No 'redis' string anywhere in application.yml/application-dev.yml/application-prod.yml (spring.cache.type and spring.data.redis.* both absent). pom.xml has spring-boot-starter-data-redis with no competing cache provider (Caffeine/Ehcache/Hazelcast/etc.), so Spring Boot 3.3.5 (confirmed in pom.xml) auto-selects Redis as cache type, defaulting the connection to localhost:6379 inside the influora-api container where no redis process runs. RedisCacheConfig.java has no CacheErrorHandler, so a RedisConnectionFailureException on cache GET propagates by Spring's default SimpleCacheErrorHandler. Three real @Cacheable sites confirmed: AdminDashboardStatsCache.pulseStats(), CreatorDiscoveryFacetsCache.computeFacets(), and HsnSacCodeService (used by InvoiceService/CampaignServiceInvoiceService/CommissionInvoiceService — a real financial invoicing path, not just a dashboard). Confirmed the CI env var mismatch (SPRING_REDIS_HOST/PORT are Boot-2 names, wrong for Boot 3.3.5) but CI's redis is exposed on 127.0.0.1:6379 which coincidentally matches Spring's own default, so CI passes regardless — supports downgrading that specific sub-item to P2 as one of the original finders already did. No runtime reproduction attempted (would require standing up a Docker environment), so evidence capped below the original score. New evidence: HsnSacCodeService is on the invoice-generation path for escrow release / commission, which the original finding only alluded to generically as 'invoice HSN lookup' — this strengthens the production-impact claim beyond dashboard-only caching.
- **Location:** deploy/utho/docker-compose.utho.yml (influora-api environment), deploy/utho/docker-compose.utho-shared.yml, deploy/hostinger/docker-compose.hostinger.yml; influora-api/src/main/java/com/influora/config/RedisCacheConfig.java; service/HsnSacCodeService.java:25 | deploy/hostinger/docker-compose.hostinger.yml (influora-api service, environment block); influora-api/src/main/java/com/influora/service/admin/AdminDashboardStatsCache.java:71 (@Cacheable); AdminDashboardService.java:103 | .github/workflows/backend-tests.yml:74-75
- **Reproduction:** Start influora-api with the utho compose env map and no SPRING_DATA_REDIS_HOST; invoke any HsnSacCodeService.resolveCode path.
- **Impact:** Deployed from the repo, invoice generation (subscription + campaign GST invoices), admin dashboard pulse and creator discovery facets would 500 on first call. Tests are green because every test path disables or never reaches the Redis cache.
- **Affected Features:** GST invoice generation (subscription + campaign), Admin dashboard pulse, Creator discovery facets
- **Agents:** perf, priya (finder models: opus, sonnet); merged from priya:PRI-01, perf:PERF-03, perf:PERF-04
- **Recommended Fix:** Add SPRING_DATA_REDIS_HOST: redis and SPRING_DATA_REDIS_PORT: '6379' to the influora-api env of all three prod composes (and a ${SPRING_DATA_REDIS_HOST} placeholder in application-prod.yml with no default so a miss fails boot), and register a CacheErrorHandler that logs and falls through so a Redis outage degrades to DB reads instead of 500s.
- **Verification Required:** Boot the utho compose locally, call GET /admin/dashboard pulse and an invoice-generating path; confirm no RedisConnectionFailureException. Check the live box env for SPRING_DATA_REDIS_HOST.
- **Finder disagreement noted at merge:** Severity differed across finders: priya=P1, perf=P1, perf=P2

### XR-PRIYA-02 - No Docker-free Spring context smoke test exists, so EV-001-class boot failures (any constructor/bean-wiring regression) are structurally invisible to a normal `mvn test` run

- **Finding:** No Docker-free Spring context smoke test exists, so EV-001-class boot failures (any constructor/bean-wiring regression) are structurally invisible to a normal `mvn test` run
- **Severity:** P1 (cross-review addition by priya; not passed through the adversarial verifier)
- **Evidence Score:** E3
- **Confidence:** 78
- **Status:** OBSERVED / COMMITTED
- **Evidence:** meera-run's own EV-001 reproduction needed a live migrated MySQL under SPRING_PROFILES_ACTIVE=dev to reach the failure point; this repo's own working memory (reference_local_mvn_test_skips_docker_tests) documents that the only full-@SpringBootTest-context tests are Testcontainers-gated and are skipped when Docker is unavailable — exactly why a constructor-injection break like TrendPullJob's could be committed and merged through multiple subsequent 'repair round' commits without mvn test ever catching it.
- **Location:** Absence across influora-api/src/test/java/com/influora — no @SpringBootTest class found that asserts context load without also requiring Testcontainers/Docker
- **Reproduction:** Not performed; code trace only (see Evidence).
- **Impact:** Every future constructor-injection or bean-wiring mistake has the same total-outage blast radius as EV-001 and the same near-zero chance of being caught pre-deploy, because this repo's only full-context tests are Testcontainers-gated and silently skip when Docker is unavailable.
- **Affected Features:** See Impact.
- **Agents:** priya (cross-review)
- **Recommended Fix:** Add one @SpringBootTest(webEnvironment=NONE) smoke test on H2/mocked DataSource (no Testcontainers) that only asserts the ApplicationContext refreshes, run on every mvn test.
- **Verification Required:** Confirm the new test fails against the pre-fix TrendPullJob and passes once @Autowired is restored, with no Docker/MySQL dependency.

- **Severity:** P1

- **Impact:** A brand or creator who needs help has no in-product way to open, read, or reply to a support ticket, despite the entire backend (routes, service, ownership scoping, entity/repository, DB tables) being built and unit-tested.
- **Affected Features:** User support tickets create/list/get/message, POST/GET /support/tickets, GET /support/tickets/{id}, POST /support/tickets/{id}/messages, admin support desk /admin/support

- **Severity:** P1

OnboardingService.submitBrandKyc (OnboardingService.java:171-183) sets PENDING and publishes no event. AdminBrandService.verifyKyc (AdminBrandService.java:280-319) is the only way to reach VERIFIED. Workspace.getKycGstinDocUrl/getKycPanDocUrl (Workspace.java:288-292) and OnboardingService.resolveKycDocUrl (:205) have no production callers (grep). || [blueprint-notifications-support-uploads:UPLOAD-01] grep -rn resolveKycDocUrl influora-api/src/main/java returns only the two methods' own declarations and their mutual javadoc cross-references -- no call site anywhere. FE call sites (KycIdentityForm.tsx, brand-kyc-prompt.tsx) confirmed to correctly persist the private-upload `key` (not the presigned preview `url`) into the onboarding record, so the document IS captured -- it just can never be displayed again by this codebase as it stands.

AdminBrandService.verifyKyc (280-320) never loads or exposes any document. OnboardingService.submitBrandKyc has zero notification/email/alert code (grep for notif|email|alert returns nothing). CampaignValidator.validateStatusForWorkspace (60-68) confirms this KYC status gates ACTIVE campaigns, so it's a real money-path dependency, not cosmetic. Static-only verification (no runtime execution), hence evidence capped at 3 despite very strong cross-file confirmation.

- **Reproduction:** Register a brand, submit KYC, try to publish: 403 WORKSPACE_NOT_VERIFIED until an admin approves. The admin brand detail shows no document links.

### EV-024 - Meera brand funding flow has no working live path: demo campaign id plus a link to a non-existent route

- **Severity:** P1
- **Evidence Score:** E4 (register E3)
- **Confidence:** 90 (register 85)
- **Status:** OBSERVED; verification verdict CONFIRMED / provenance COMMITTED
- **Evidence:** [kavya:KAV-01] MeeraWorkspace.tsx:21 `const MEERA_DEMO_CAMPAIGN_ID = 'meera_demo_campaign'` and :76-77 `api.payments.fundEscrow(MEERA_DEMO_CAMPAIGN_ID, idempotencyKey)`, wired to StageFunding's Pay CTA (StageFunding.tsx:120) in live mode too. EscrowController.fund -> EscrowService.deriveFundAmount findByIdAndWorkspaceId(...).orElseThrow returns 404. RequestPaymentExecutor.confirmActionUrl:181-183 returns '/brand/escrow/fund?campaignIntentId=...', which ToolResultRenderer.tsx:283-290 renders as a 'Confirm payment' link in a new tab, and App.tsx has no /brand/escrow route (falls to NotFoundPage at :883). handlePay has no try/catch. The idempotency key is `${id}-${Date.now()}`, a new key per click, so it provides no double-click protection.
- **Adversarial verification:** MeeraWorkspace.tsx:21 hardcodes MEERA_DEMO_CAMPAIGN_ID with its own comment admitting it must be replaced with a real campaign id in live mode; handlePay (lines 75-81) unconditionally uses it, wired to onPay in the live-mounted <LivingCanvas> (lines 111, 137). brand-meera.tsx renders <MeeraWorkspace/> with no gating, mounted unconditionally at /brand/meera in App.tsx inside the normal authenticated brand route tree. Separately, RequestPaymentExecutor.java:181-183 returns '/brand/escrow/fund?campaignIntentId=...' which ToolResultRenderer.tsx:283-292 renders as a real clickable <a target=_blank> link; grepping every '/brand/...' <Route> in App.tsx (30+ routes) shows no '/brand/escrow' route exists, so it resolves to the catch-all <Route path="*" element={<NotFoundPage/>}> at App.tsx:883. MeeraInternalController.java:245,257-258 confirms POST /request_payment is a live, tool-gated (requireTool) endpoint actually reachable by the production Meera pipeline, not dead code. New evidence: Traced one step further than the original claim: confirmed via full router enumeration in App.tsx that no route resolves the confirm link (definite 404 to NotFoundPage), and confirmed via MeeraInternalController that request_payment is a live, gated, reachable tool -- ruling out the possibility this is unregistered/inert code.
- **Location:** src/components/feature/meera/MeeraWorkspace.tsx:21,42,75-80; influora-api/src/main/java/com/influora/service/meera/tool/RequestPaymentExecutor.java:181-183; src/components/feature/meera/ToolResultRenderer.tsx:283-290; src/App.tsx:325-331
- **Reproduction:** Staging, live mode: /brand/meera -> reach funding stage after request_payment -> click Pay -> network shows campaignId=meera_demo_campaign -> 404 CAMPAIGN_NOT_FOUND.
- **Impact:** The brand's Meera 'Fund & go live' either 404s silently (unhandled rejection) or opens a 404 page. Campaigns cannot be funded from the Meera surface, so confirm_launch always fails ESCROW_NOT_FUNDED unless the brand funds elsewhere.
- **Affected Features:** Meera brand workspace funding, Meera request_payment tool card, Meera confirm_launch
- **Agents:** kavya (finder models: opus); merged from kavya:KAV-01
- **Recommended Fix:** Carry the real campaignId (from the create_campaign tool result or the CampaignIntent) into MeeraWorkspace. Replace handlePay with the existing useEscrowFund hook / FundEscrowButton (which already polls server state). Point confirmActionUrl at a real route (for example /brand/campaigns/:id?fund=1) or add the route. Generate the idempotency key once per intent, not per click. Add a vitest that asserts handlePay never sends MEERA_DEMO_CAMPAIGN_ID when isApiLive().
- **Verification Required:** Meera/Neha: in staging, create a campaign via Meera, click Pay, and observe the POST /wallet/escrow/fund body and response; click 'Confirm payment' and observe the route.

### EV-031 - 'TDS shown on invoices' / 'TDS on payouts' is not computed anywhere in the automated (real, gateway-driven) creator payout path

- **Finding:** 'TDS shown on invoices' / 'TDS on payouts' is not computed anywhere in the automated (real, gateway-driven) creator payout path
- **Severity:** P1
- **Evidence Score:** E3
- **Confidence:** 88 (register 74)
- **Status:** **CLOSED (COPY PART) — FIXED IN COMMITTED CODE 51577f5, VERIFIED FROM THE COMMIT.** On the audited RC 115f698 itself, still OBSERVED as originally found; verification verdict CONFIRMED / provenance COMMITTED. Committed 2026-09-20 to `feat/meera-creator-phase-e` (part of the same six-commit wave as EV-007/XR-ROHAN-01, not pushed) — see the 2026-09-20 Remediation log entry above. New evidence score/confidence: E4/92.
- **2026-09-20 remediation:** the "TDS shown on invoices" / "TDS on payouts" and Form 16A claims were removed from customer-facing copy (including `creator-wallet.tsx`, the specific instance this finding's adversarial-verification step cited). **Explicitly still open, and out of scope for this fix:** `Payout.createPending`/`createQueued` still take no `tdsAmount` parameter, so automated TDS computation is still not implemented for the real, gateway-driven payout path — only the false claim about it was removed, not the underlying capability gap.
- **Evidence:** [tejas:TEJAS-02] Payout.createPending (used by PayoutService, the real RazorpayX payout path) and Payout.createQueued (used by WalletService) accept no tdsAmount parameter, so the column stays null for every automated payout. Only Payout.createManualPaid — called exclusively from AdminFinanceService for admin-entered, out-of-band bank transfers — ever sets tdsAmount. No TDS-calculation logic exists in PayoutService.java, PlatformFeeService.java, or CampaignServiceInvoiceService.java.
- **Adversarial verification:** Payout.java: tdsAmount (84-85) defaults null, set ONLY inside createManualPaid (218/235). createPending (129-151) and createQueued (165-189) take no tdsAmount param. Confirmed PayoutService.java:463 calls createPending (automated gateway payout) and WalletService.java:402 calls createQueued (creator withdrawal) -- neither can ever record TDS. Only AdminFinanceService.java:230 (admin manual payout, fed by an admin-typed number with just a range check, AdminFinanceService.java:179-183) ever sets it. Went further than the cited files: src/pages/creator-wallet.tsx:978 states as fact 'TDS (1%) is deducted at source as per IT Act. Download Form 16A quarterly for filing' directly above the same tab's own admitted empty state ('No tax-document endpoint exists yet'); grepped all of influora-api and found zero Form16A generation code anywhere. The 0828/CA-gate ruling explains descoping automatic computation, it does not excuse the hardcoded false UI claim -- confirms the finder's 'the defect is the claim' exactly, with a second independently-found instance. New evidence: creator-wallet.tsx:978's factual TDS/Form-16A claim is directly contradicted by the same screen's own empty-state copy and by the total absence of any Form16A backend code -- a second concrete instance of the misrepresentation.
- **Location:** influora-api/src/main/java/com/influora/domain/entity/Payout.java:84-85,126-171,172-190,205-240; influora-api/src/main/java/com/influora/service/PayoutService.java:463; influora-api/src/main/java/com/influora/service/WalletService.java:402; influora-api/src/main/java/com/influora/service/admin/Admin
- **Reproduction:** See evidence; reproduce by reading the cited source lines at RC 115f698.
- **Impact:** A brand or creator relying on 'TDS on payouts' as an active platform behavior (rather than an admin-manual exception) may believe Section 194-O withholding is being handled automatically when it is not for the normal, automated deal flow — a tax-compliance and trust-integrity gap, not just a copy nuance.
- **Affected Features:** 
- **Agents:** tejas (finder models: sonnet); merged from tejas:TEJAS-02
- **Recommended Fix:** Either implement real TDS computation in the automated payout path (PayoutService/PlatformFeeService) so tdsAmount is genuinely populated on every gateway payout, or change customer-facing copy to accurately describe current behavior (e.g., only guarantee TDS handling for the flows that actually compute it) until the automated path exists.
- **Verification Required:** Confirm with backend/finance whether TDS withholding happens in a downstream system this repo doesn't represent (e.g., a manual CA/accounting reconciliation) — if so, the customer-facing promise of automatic, per-payout TDS handling is still unsupported by the product itself and should be corrected regardless.
- **Finder disagreement noted at merge:** Prior ruling (fix wave 0828) scoped automatic TDS out pending spec + CA gate; the defect is the customer-facing TDS claim, not the missing computation.

### XR-ROHAN-01 - secure-payments.tsx (the flagship payment-trust page) lacks the sibling pages' banned-phrase governance comment and independently makes an unconditional 'Guaranteed payment' claim that EV-015's code path falsifies

- **Finding:** secure-payments.tsx (the flagship payment-trust page) lacks the sibling pages' banned-phrase governance comment and independently makes an unconditional 'Guaranteed payment' claim that EV-015's code path falsifies
- **Severity:** P1 (cross-review addition by rohan+tejas; not passed through the adversarial verifier)
- **Evidence Score:** E3
- **Confidence:** 75
- **Status:** **CLOSED — FIXED IN COMMITTED CODE 51577f5, VERIFIED FROM THE COMMIT.** On the audited RC 115f698 itself, still OBSERVED as originally found; provenance COMMITTED. Committed 2026-09-20 to `feat/meera-creator-phase-e` (part of the same six-commit wave as EV-007/EV-031, not pushed) — see the 2026-09-20 Remediation log entry above. New evidence score/confidence: E4/88.
- **2026-09-20 remediation:** the unconditional "Guaranteed payment" claim and the repeated RBI-Payment-Aggregator language on `secure-payments.tsx` were removed along with the rest of the EV-007/EV-031 copy fixes in the same commit. This claim's own falsifying condition (EV-015 — a brand could refund a FUNDED hold after approval) is independently CLOSED in the companion commit `a76ea77` from the same wave, so the claim is no longer contradicted by a still-open code path either.
- **Evidence:** secure-payments.tsx (renamed from /features/escrow per its own comment at lines 4-6) has no banned-phrase/governance comment block, unlike hype.tsx:41-49, deal-room.tsx:45, and how-it-works-brands.tsx:43,446, which all carry the 2026-09-02 ruling banning 'escrow' and unmeasured/guarantee-style claims. secure-payments.tsx:36 nonetheless reads: "Guaranteed payment. The brand's money is already locked and protected before you film a single reel..." -- an unconditional promise to creators, directly contradicted by EscrowService.java:946-988's refund() path (brand OWNER/ADMIN can refund a FUNDED hold with no delivery/approval/dispute precondition, per the code's own comment at ~line 898 that the dispute check is deliberately withheld -- matches EV-015) and undercut by EV-002's fund-integrity findings on the same FUNDED state.
- **Location:** src/pages/features/secure-payments.tsx:4-6 (missing governance header), :36 ('Guaranteed payment'); cross-referenced against influora-api/src/main/java/com/influora/service/EscrowService.java:898,946-988
- **Reproduction:** Not performed; code trace only (see Evidence).
- **Impact:** This is the highest financial-trust page for creators, the audience with least leverage if a brand pulls funds back. An absolute 'guaranteed' promise the platform's own code can void unilaterally is a direct consumer-claims liability, and it slipped through precisely because this page alone lacks the banned-phrase review its siblings have.
- **Affected Features:** See Impact.
- **Agents:** rohan+tejas (cross-review)
- **Recommended Fix:** Add the same governance comment block to secure-payments.tsx and re-audit its copy (it also repeats the RBI-Payment-Aggregator claim at lines 97, 210 per tejas.json). Replace the unconditional 'Guaranteed payment' line with delivery/approval-conditioned language disclosing the dispute/refund path, consistent with terms-of-service.md:28's own facilitator disclaimer. Treat EV-015's fix as a prerequisite for this claim being true at all.
- **Verification Required:** After fixes, grep all marketing pages for 'guarant' and confirm each hit has a conditioning clause or documented exemption; extend the citations.rc proof-os gate to fail if a new src/pages/features/ page lacks the banned-phrase comment block its siblings carry.

### EV-032 - Hype solid color critical fail (2.43:1 contrast)

- **Finding:** Hype solid color critical fail (2.43:1 contrast)
- **Severity:** P1
- **Evidence Score:** E3
- **Confidence:** 80 (register 70)
- **Status:** OBSERVED; verification verdict CONFIRMED / provenance COMMITTED
- **Evidence:** [zara:ZARA-002] #06b6d4 (hype-solid) on white = 2.43:1 FAIL
- **Adversarial verification:** Confirmed globals.css:101 (#06b6d4 light) and :171 (#22d3ee dark). Independently recomputed WCAG contrast for #06b6d4 vs white by hand (relative luminance 0.38276 -> ratio 2.426:1), matching the finder's 2.43 almost exactly -- fails AA for text (4.5:1) and even the 3:1 UI/large-text floor. But grepping all real usages of hype-solid in src/**/*.tsx found NO case of it used as text color on white -- every real usage is `bg-hype-solid text-white` on live CTAs (landing.tsx:503 hero button, hype-inbox-card.tsx:95, FunnelCta.tsx:81, brand-new-hype-campaign.tsx:646) or non-text decoration. Since contrast ratio is symmetric, the same 2.43:1 failure applies to the actual pairing (white text on hype-solid background); I also computed the dark variant (#22d3ee vs white) independently at 1.807:1, an even worse failure. The underlying defect is real and reachable on multiple live buttons in both themes; the finder's stated color-role (text-on-white) is not what ships, but the numeric failure and its production impact are confirmed, if anything more broadly than claimed. New evidence: No code renders hype-solid as text on white; the real reachable defect is white button-label text ON a hype-solid background across several live CTAs, with the identical 2.43:1 (light) / newly-computed 1.807:1 (dark) contrast failure.
- **Location:** src/app/globals.css:101
- **Reproduction:** See evidence; reproduce by reading the cited source lines at RC 115f698.
- **Impact:** Per the CEO readiness matrix (UX row): hype-solid primary CTAs (landing hero, FunnelCta and others) render white text at 2.43:1 in light mode and 1.81:1 in dark mode, failing even the 3:1 large-text floor; this breaches the standing rule that CTAs must be WCAG-AA.
- **Affected Features:** 
- **Agents:** zara (finder models: haiku); merged from zara:ZARA-002
- **Recommended Fix:** Use --hype-foreground or darken to #0570a6
- **Verification Required:** Re-measure hype-solid with white text in light and dark themes and confirm >= 4.5:1 (normal text) after the token change.
- **Finder disagreement noted at merge:** Single haiku finder with no evidence score; Arjun confirmed src/app/globals.css:101 defines --hype-solid: #06b6d4. Contrast ratio not independently measured.

## 39. P3 Findings

Seventy P3s, all listed so that small issues do not disappear. Four are positive or informational controls, kept to prevent regressions: EV-117, EV-118, EV-120 and EV-131.

**Not counted:**
- **EV-034 (REFUTED):** "deploy/utho proxy chain collapses client IPs". The fix is present and tested.
- **EV-026 (EXCLUDED, UNRESOLVED):** it describes uncommitted F-0857/F-0858 code.

## 40. Missing Features

These are capabilities that are absent, or present but unreachable, in RC 115f698. "Absent" means no implementing code was found. "Unwired" means the code exists but no user can reach it. "Off" means it is disabled by default in every committed config.

| Capability | State | Needed for | EV |
|---|---|---|---|
| Server-side guard: one active escrow hold per milestone | Absent (F-0873 partial, uncommitted) | Payment integrity | EV-002, EV-098 |
| Brand refund policy enforcement (no refund after approval except via dispute) | Absent | Payment integrity; "payment protected" claims | EV-015 |
| Single campaign-activation guard | Absent in RC (F-0848 uncommitted) | Fee and escrow integrity | EV-005, EV-074 |
| Approval releases payment (deliverable linked to milestone) | Absent | Creator trust; product loop | EV-019 |
| Auto-release | Absent | Creator trust | EV-019 |
| Self-serve creator bank payout | Off (UI flag only; backend broken by EV-014/EV-003) | Creator money-out | EV-020, EV-014, EV-003 |
| Server-side PAYOUTS flag and RazorpayX placeholder detection | Absent | Safe money-out | EV-020 |
| Segregated PA custody (Razorpay Route or nodal) | Absent | Truthful custody claim or a licence path | EV-007 |
| Automated TDS on payouts; Form 16A | Absent | Tax compliance; truthful copy | EV-031 |
| Brand wallet withdrawal or refund to source | Absent | Brand trust; stored-value exposure | EV-086 |
| Admin view of KYC documents; KYC-submitted notification | Unwired | Brand activation | EV-016 |
| Requester support tickets UI | Unwired (backend built and tested) | Any user support channel | EV-012 |
| Meera credit meter and paywall UI | Unwired | AI metering transparency | EV-065 |
| Contract cancel and amend UI | Unwired | Deal lifecycle | EV-041 |
| Creator identity KYC verification | Absent (self-declared) | Payout risk | EV-058 |
| Chargeback and refund event handling | Absent | Money-in integrity | EV-094 |
| Gateway fee accounting | Absent | Revenue truth | EV-090 |
| Per-workspace AI hard cap in prod | Off | AI availability for all tenants | EV-044 |
| Actor attribution on money moves | Absent | Disputes, fraud investigation | XR-KABIR-01 |
| DB backups with a tested restore | Absent | Ledger disaster recovery | EV-093 |
| Metrics and alerting | Absent | Operations | EV-088 |
| Trend ingestion (TrendSpark) | Off and fails closed | TrendSpark nudges | EV-013 |
| Creator Copilot batch jobs; connect nudges | Off | Retention hooks | EV-068, EV-150 |
| Custom-link click tracking | Absent (`linkClicks` always empty) | Portfolio analytics | EV-147 |
| Newsletter subscription | Absent | Retention and content | EV-049 |
| Bulk admin email | 501 | Admin comms | EV-050 |
| Postiz social scheduling | Absent (docs only) | Content ops | EV-107 |
| Content calendar | Absent | Content ops | EV-123 |
| Appeal subsystem for suspensions | Absent (status filter is a no-op) | Moderation | blueprint-admin |
| Marketing acquisition metrics (admin) | Not built (documented) | Admin growth dashboard | blueprint-admin |
| FAQPage and Product structured data | Absent | SEO | EV-158 |
| Manual dark-mode toggle | Absent | UX | EV-172 |
| Refresh-token reuse detection; revoked-token purge | Absent | Auth hardening | EV-129, EV-127 |
| JWT secret rotation with a dual-key window | Absent | Operations | q-answers |

## 41. Missing Tests

Every test below should **fail on the RC and pass after the fix**. That is the standing "falsify the gate" rule. Execution belongs to Kabir or Meera, not review by analysis.

| # | Missing test | Why | EV |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Docker-free `@SpringBootTest(webEnvironment=NONE)` context-load smoke test on every `mvn test` | The boot failure shipped invisibly | EV-001, XR-PRIYA-02 |
| 2 | Re-fund of a FUNDED or RELEASED milestone is rejected; a DB unique-active-hold constraint test | Double debit | EV-002 |
| 3 | Brand refund after submit or approve is rejected (non-dispute path) | Self-refund | EV-015 |
| 4 | `POST /campaigns` with `status=ACTIVE` and no funded escrow is rejected, or forced to DRAFT | Unfunded publish | EV-005 |
| 5 | A percent-encoded `/internal/...` path returns 401/403 through the full filter chain (Spring slice), plus an architecture test that bans raw `getRequestURI()` in security filters | Internal bypass | EV-004, XR-PRIYA-01 |
| 6 | A VIEWER approving a deliverable is rejected | Unauthorized release | EV-021 |
| 7 | Approve with a funded milestone releases (after #6) | Approval-release | EV-019 |

EV-175: the identical defect in 3 sibling TrendSourceClient beans blocked boot one bean past EV-001. 2026-09-19: both fixes applied together, first boot-verified in an uncommitted worktree (3/3 "Started"), then **committed as `fb6d267`** (parent `7846d93`, not pushed) and **re-verified from that commit**: `mvn clean test` 3,730/0/0 (22 skipped) plus a real `spring-boot:run` boot that **reached "Started InfluoraApiApplication in 49.231 seconds"** (dev profile, fresh MySQL 8, no Docker) with 0 matches for BeanCreationException in the boot log, and correctly served `/api/v1/health` (200 UP), `/api/v1/config/public` (200), `/api/v1/campaigns`/`/api/v1/wallet` (401) — see the third Remediation log entry. Kavya independently re-reviewed the committed diff and APPROVEd it. **EV-001 and EV-175 are CLOSED.** `fb6d267` is committed locally only — not pushed, no new RC cut from it. XR-PRIYA-02: no Docker-free context-load test exists, so bean-wiring breaks are invisible to mvn test — this is exactly why EV-001 and EV-175 both shipped undetected, and this gap is still open. ## 46. Post-Launch Monitoring

These recommendations follow from the findings. None of them exists today (EV-088). Each item says what it would catch.

| Signal | Why (finding it would catch) | Suggested alert |
|---|---|---|
| API liveness and 5xx rate | EV-001-class boot or wiring failures; unhealthy containers are not restarted (q-answers) | Any failed health probe for 2 minutes; 5xx above baseline |
| Platform clearing-wallet balance | Affiliate liabilities with no brand debit (EV-033); negative-balance exemption | Balance below threshold, or a negative delta not matched by releases |
| Escrow holds per milestone greater than 1 while active | Double-fund (EV-002) | Any occurrence |
| Refunds after deliverable approval | Self-refund (EV-015) until fixed | Any occurrence, reviewed daily |
| Payout orphaned-debit sweep repairs; payouts stuck in PENDING | EV-003, EV-035 | Any repair; PENDING older than SLA |
| Top-up reconciliation repairs and 7-day give-ups | EV-145; webhook misses | Any repair |
| `escrow_invoice_failures` exhausted rows | GST Doc#2 loss (q-answers) | Any EXHAUSTED row |
| Email outbox FAILED and backlog | EmailWorker (EV-115) | Backlog age; FAILED count |
| AI global spend vs the $15/day ceiling, per workspace | EV-044 (one tenant starving the rest) | 70% of ceiling; any one workspace above 30% |
| influora-ai `/readyz` plus per-router presence | EV-040 (router silently missing) | Missing router |
| Redis cache errors | EV-017 | Any `RedisConnectionFailureException` |
| Async handler exceptions | EV-046 (no handler) | Error count |
| Outbound client latency and timeouts (Meta, Shopify, Gemini, RazorpayX) | EV-045 | p95 above threshold |
| Auth anomalies: admin login failures, MFA re-enrolments | EV-022, EV-023 | Any MFA re-enrol; lockout bursts |
| `/internal/**` requests not from the AI service | EV-004 until fixed | Any occurrence |
| Rate-limit map size | EV-114 (never evicted) | Growth trend |
| Live bundle hash vs the released build | EV-010 (live differs from repo) | Mismatch after deploy |
| KYC queue age; manual payout queue age | EV-016, EV-020 (ops-dependent beta) | Older than the same-day SLA |

## 47. Brand Presentation Findings

What can and cannot be told to brands today, based on the evidence.

**Can be said (code-verified mechanisms, not runtime-proven on this RC):**
- A Deal Room with negotiation, e-signed contracts and SSE messaging.
- Server-derived HYPE economics.
- A human confirmation before Meera moves money.
- Signature-verified sales-tracking webhooks from the brand's own store.
- Meta-synced creator stats. This is only accurate after the EV-008 fix ships and is re-verified.
- A fee charged once at publish, and Pro at Rs 4,999/month.

**Must not be said:**
- That an RBI-authorized PA holds brand funds, or that Influora never pools them (EV-007).
- That payment is "guaranteed" or "protected" in any unconditional way (XR-ROHAN-01, EV-015).
- That approving a deliverable pays the creator. A separate release is needed (EV-019).
- That the refund policy is final (EV-028).

**Must be disclosed to beta brands:**
- The publish fee is 10% of the **maximum** budget, charged upfront and non-refundable (EV-087).
- Wallet money cannot be withdrawn (EV-086).
- KYC approval is manual (EV-016).
- Support is by direct contact only, because there is no in-app support channel (EV-012).
- The Meera "pay" CTA is broken (EV-024) and must be hidden or fixed before any brand demo.

**Presentation quality:**
- Hype-solid CTAs fail contrast (EV-032).
- A mock theme appears on `/brand/meera` (EV-043).
- Admin at-risk metrics show hardcoded zeros (EV-043).

## 48. Creator Presentation Findings

**Can be said:**
- Joining is free, and the commission is taken only when a deal pays out (the `deductAtRelease` call site is confirmed).
- Contracts are e-signed.
- Deliverable uploads are validated.
- There are two-way reviews.
- The portfolio page is built from Meta-synced stats, once EV-008 is closed.

**Must not be said:**
- "Guaranteed payment" or "guaranteed the rate" (XR-ROHAN-01; hype.tsx:99). A brand can refund a funded hold even after approval (EV-015).
- "Payout typically inside 24 hours" (EV-104). Withdraw is off, payouts are manual SUPER_ADMIN transfers (EV-020), and the automated path fails for every withdrawal (EV-014).
- "TDS invoice generated for you" or "TDS on payouts" for normal payouts (EV-031).
- Affiliate commission earnings, because settlement cannot post (RC-16).

**Must be disclosed to beta creators:**
- Payment depends on the brand clicking release (EV-019).
- Payout is manual, by bank transfer with a UTR, on an ops SLA (EV-020).
- Identity KYC is self-declared (EV-058).

**Presentation risk:** The landing-page "Ananya" portfolio card reads as a real creator testimonial and needs an "illustrative" label (EV-169).

## 49. Investor Communication Findings

**Can be stated as code-verified fact** (static, E3, not runtime-proven on this RC):
- The three-service architecture.
- The monetization mechanics: 10% (7% Pro) publish fee, 15% creator commission, Rs 4,999/month Pro, and AI credits.
- AI cost governance: a $15/day global ceiling, a kill switch, and a per-creator cap.
- Webhook-verified sales attribution.
- Meta-synced analytics (after the EV-008 fix).
- An extensive automated test suite (3,621 backend, 1,348 frontend and 874 AI tests passing), **with the caveat** that full-context and real-DB tests did not run and that mocked money tests missed real DB defects.

**Must not be stated:**
- Any traction, customer count, GMV, revenue or market size. None exists in the audit inputs, and fabricated homepage statistics were already removed for legal reasons.
- AI accuracy or quality figures: no live-model evals exist (EV-057).
- That the platform is live-ready, or that payments are licensed or segregated (EV-007).
- That payouts are automated (EV-020).

**Material risks an investor would need to hear:**
1. The current RC cannot boot (EV-001). This is a release-process failure: there is no context test, and images are ungated (EV-063).
2. The custody claim is a regulatory exposure, compounded by a Razorpay account shared with another product (EV-144, severity disputed). The PA licence is deferred.
3. There are no DB backups for the ledger (EV-093).
4. Operations are manual for KYC and payouts.
5. The deploy is not reproducible from the repo (EV-010).

**Reasonable framing:** Pre-launch. The core product mechanics are implemented. A controlled beta is gated on a defined, evidence-backed fix list (section 51).

## 50. Final Production Readiness Assessment

**Recomputed 2026-09-20**, after the six-lane P0 wave (`be76665`, `9bb8055`, `6228d61`, `51577f5`, `c6ed523`, `a76ea77`, on top of `de249ed`/`fb6d267`). This supersedes the original 2026-09-19 verdict below it does not delete; the original reasoning is kept for the record.

**What do we know, as of 2026-09-20?**
- All **nine** of the originally-audited P0s that a fix could close are now **CLOSED — FIXED IN COMMITTED CODE, VERIFIED FROM THE COMMIT**: EV-001 and EV-175 (boot, `fb6d267`), EV-002 (double-fund, `de249ed`), EV-004 (internal-path bypass, `be76665`), EV-005 (create-to-ACTIVE, `9bb8055`), EV-006 code part (`6228d61`), EV-007 copy part plus EV-031/XR-ROHAN-01 (`51577f5`), EV-008 (`c6ed523`), and EV-015 (`a76ea77`). **The only P0 left open in the register is XR-PRIYA-01** (the systemic no-shared-decode-utility/no-architecture-test gap — not in this wave's close list, even though `be76665`'s new `RequestPaths.java` may bear on it).
- **None of this is on origin or in production.** Nine commits sit on `feat/meera-creator-phase-e`, locally, not pushed; no new RC has been cut; nothing has been deployed. The currently-live build is unaffected by any of this work.
- **Hard blocker before push:** `6228d61`'s own diff re-displays all six real credential values (Anthropic, Sarvam, both R2 keys, both MSG91 keys) on the removed side of its `env.example` hunks; they are still valid and still in `origin`'s history. Rotating every one, in its provider console, is a prerequisite to pushing this branch at all — not merely a nice-to-have.
- **Unproven-in-this-environment gaps, not defects:** the 23 Docker-gated Testcontainers suites never ran locally, including `CampaignActivationLedgerIntegrationTest` (EV-005's exactly-once-publish-fee ledger invariant is therefore still unproven) and the Testcontainers snapshot proof EV-178 still owes.
- **Explicitly still open, unrelated to or deliberately out of scope for this wave:** EV-079 (service-token replay, needs a coordinated Python change); EV-019/EV-021 (approval still does not release money, and any workspace member including VIEWER can still approve — the authorization gap is masked only as long as EV-019 stays broken); EV-181 (every escrow fund stalls ~50s and silently drops its history event); the approve-vs-refund deadlock EV-015's own reviewers flagged (a brand can decline both approve and release, stalling a creator indefinitely, without ever touching the new dispute-only refund gate); the unverified "payouts typically inside 24 hours" marketing claim; and the EV-007 items code cannot close (who legally holds wallet balances/insolvency treatment, TDS applicability, the RBI-KYC privacy-policy citation, the stale OG image) — these need a lawyer, not a commit.
- Money-out and affiliate settlement remain unfixed and untouched (EV-014, EV-003, EV-011, EV-025, EV-033) — none of these were in this wave's scope.
- Positively: the schema applies, the full six-lane suite is green (3,915 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors, 23 skipped — cross-checked against the surefire XML), `tsc`/`vite build` are clean, the packaged jar boots and serves live HTTP with each of the four live-probed lanes (EV-004, EV-005, EV-008, EV-015) showing a genuine refusal with a working control, not a blanket deny.

**How do we know?**
- Runtime: meera-run boot attempts; the verifier's bean-factory probe; Flyway on real MySQL; all suites executed; Kabir's library probe; Meera-DB's scratch repro; live GETs (EV-009, EV-042).
- **2026-09-19/20 remediation-specific:** commit-level `mvn clean test` runs cross-checked against surefire XML report counts; real-MySQL concurrency races (EV-002/EV-176/EV-177, 7/7 clean); packaged-jar boots from committed code with live HTTP probes and controls (EV-004, EV-005, EV-008, EV-015); static gates (`ci/secret-scan.py`, `ev007-regulated-claims.test.ts`); Kavya's per-commit diff-scope review plus `git merge-base --is-ancestor` checks ruling out the two earlier partial-revert attempts (`ccc9e6a`, `2d5992b`).
- Code traces with file:line citations.
- 36 adversarial verdicts (original audit).
- 4 cross-team reviews.

**How strong is the evidence?**
- E5 for boot (EV-001/EV-175, commit-verified) and for SEO.
- **E4-E5 for the six-lane wave's closed P0s** — commit-level `mvn test` + surefire cross-check for all six, plus live HTTP probes with controls for four of the six (EV-004, EV-005, EV-008, EV-015); EV-006 and EV-007/EV-031/XR-ROHAN-01 are matched by static gates rather than a live exploit probe.
- E3-E4 for the still-open money-out/affiliate/authorization defects: code-traced and unit-repro'd, never exercised end to end.
- E2 for performance and business assumptions.
- Overall **E4** as of 2026-09-20 (up from E3 at the original audit).

**How confident are we?**
- **90** as of 2026-09-20 (up from 75 at the original audit) — driven by nine independently commit-verified P0 closures with cross-checked test evidence and, for four of the six 2026-09-20 lanes, live HTTP probes with controls.
- Held down from higher by: nothing pushed or deployed yet; the Testcontainers-only invariants (EV-005's ledger, EV-178's snapshot proof) still unproven in this environment; EV-006 rotation, EV-007 legal sign-off and XR-PRIYA-01 all still open; EV-008 verified live on one surface of several the same commit touched. The original 36 adversarial verdicts and their limits (section 5) still apply to everything this wave did not touch.

**What is unknown?**
- Whether the 23 skipped Testcontainers suites (including `CampaignActivationLedgerIntegrationTest`) actually pass in a real Docker/CI run.
- Real CI status on this branch (never pushed, so no CI run exists yet).
- Behaviour of EV-008's admin/discovery/Meera surfaces, which were unit-tested but not live-probed.
- Live env values and key liveness beyond what the commit messages disclose.
- The live compose and edge headers.
- Model quality; performance under load.
- Real brand and creator acceptance of the fee model and the manual operations.
- The contents of the next RC once this branch is pushed and cut (EV-018-class drift risk remains until main is frozen).

**What happens next?**
- Rotate all six EV-006 credentials, in every provider console, before this branch is ever pushed.
- Push `feat/meera-creator-phase-e` and cut a new RC from it; re-run the same boot/probe sequence from that RC.
- Run the 23 skipped Testcontainers suites in real Docker/CI, including `CampaignActivationLedgerIntegrationTest` and an EV-178 REPEATABLE-READ proof.
- Get legal/counsel sign-off on the remaining EV-007 items.
- Explicitly close or scope out XR-PRIYA-01, EV-079, EV-019/EV-021, EV-181 and the approve-vs-refund deadlock before real outside money is exposed.
- Then consider a limited, invite-only beta with creator withdrawals off server-side, affiliate settlement disabled, TrendSpark out of scope and no paid acquisition — see the Go-Live Checklist (§45) and Recommended Next Actions (§51) for the itemised sequence.

**Readiness verdict, 2026-09-20: CONDITIONALLY READY for a controlled beta, pending the five items above. Decision: CONDITIONAL GO-LIVE (invite-only beta), gated. E4 / C90.**

### Original 2026-09-19 assessment (superseded above, kept for the record)

**What do we know?**
- RC 115f698 cannot start (EV-001). Any deploy of RC 115f698 is a total backend outage. **2026-09-19 update:** an EV-001 fix was built, tested and boot-verified, first only in an uncommitted worktree (not on the RC, not on main), and was not sufficient on its own: with it applied, boot still failed on 3 sibling TrendSourceClient beans carrying the identical defect (new finding EV-175, P0). **2026-09-19, commit `fb6d267`:** both fixes were then committed to `feat/meera-creator-phase-e` (parent `7846d93`, not pushed) and re-verified from a clean checkout of that commit — `mvn clean test` (3,730/0/0, 22 skipped) and a real `spring-boot:run` boot reached "Started InfluoraApiApplication," with health/config/auth-gated probes all correct — see the third Remediation log entry. **EV-001 and EV-175 are now CLOSED.** The backend boots today from committed code (`fb6d267`), but that commit has not been pushed and no new RC has been cut from it, so this is not yet proven on any deployable artifact.
- Even with boot fixed, three confirmed P0 money-integrity defects (EV-002, EV-015, EV-005) and a masked authorization defect (EV-021 behind EV-019) mean real brand and creator money is not safe.
- Money-out and affiliate settlement cannot work (EV-014, EV-003, EV-011, EV-025, EV-033).
- The live site makes a false regulated custody claim (EV-007, XR-ROHAN-01).
- Committed live-format keys need rotation (EV-006).
- The internal service gate can be bypassed (EV-004).
- Self-declared metrics display as verified (EV-008; fixed after the RC).
- Positively: the schema applies, 3,621 unit tests pass, money-in is well built, and the core security primitives are sound.

**How do we know?**
- Runtime: meera-run boot attempts; the verifier's bean-factory probe; Flyway on real MySQL; all suites executed; Kabir's library probe; Meera-DB's scratch repro; live GETs (EV-009, EV-042).
- Code traces with file:line citations.
- 36 adversarial verdicts.
- 4 cross-team reviews.

**How strong is the evidence?**
- E5 for the NO-GO basis (EV-001), for SEO, and for the backend test state.
- E3-E4 for the money defects: code-traced and unit-repro'd, never exercised end to end.
- E2 for performance and business assumptions.
- Overall **E3**.

**How confident are we?**
- About **97** that NO-GO is right.
- **75** that the register is a complete and correctly weighted picture. The limits are listed in section 5.

**What is unknown?**
- Behaviour after boot is fixed. No request has ever been served on this RC.
- Real CI status.
- The Testcontainers suites' results.
- Live env values and key liveness.
- The live compose and edge headers.
- Model quality.
- Performance under load.
- Real brand and creator acceptance of the fee model and the manual operations.
- Whether the post-RC EV-008 fixes are complete.
- The contents of the next RC, which is undefined until main is frozen (EV-018).

**What happens next?**
- Execute the must-fix list (section 51).
- Cut a clean RC.
- Prove boot and one full money cycle on staging with executed tests.
- Then consider a limited invite-only beta with money-out and affiliate off.

**Readiness verdict (original, 2026-09-19): NOT READY. Decision: NO-GO. E3 / C75.**
